What you will practice · Choose your pace

What you will practice

  • Clarify an account-opening requirement, distinguish ownership from signing authority, and give a useful next step.
  • Write a neutral internal handoff and preserve confidential reporting boundaries.
  • Escalate a document mismatch without silently changing the evidence or authorizing a drawdown.
  • Give a payments-queue handoff with reconciled counts, clear stage labels, and no unsupported settlement claim.
  • Correct conflicting service messages while separating dispute intake, investigation, temporary credit, and the final outcome.

Choose your pace

Quick practice · 15 minutes: complete one short exchange, check the explanations, and read both roles aloud.

Extended practice · two or three sittings: check the case facts, study the vocabulary and phrases, complete the longer conversation, then rehearse the follow-up exchange.

Level guide: designed for intermediate to advanced learners. B1 learners can start with the short exchanges and supplied phrases; B2 learners can complete the cloze conversations; C1 learners can focus on the distinctions in the language checks. These are teaching suggestions, not a certified level assessment.

Prompts

All banks, customers, accounts, transactions, figures, and local procedures are fictional. This book teaches professional English, not banking, legal, financial, compliance, or investigation advice. Actual obligations depend on the product, jurisdiction, facts, and current requirements. No exercise authorizes an account opening, fund transfer, record alteration, or disclosure of confidential reporting information.

01Account Opening and KYCExplain the missing ownership information without promising approval+

Your goal

Clarify an account-opening requirement, distinguish ownership from signing authority, and give a useful next step.

This lesson in the book, page 4 ↗

A Read the situation

Explain the missing ownership information without promising approval

Application A62 is the first business-account application by Northbank Design at this fictional bank. Its authorized-signatory details are supplied, but the ownership information requested for the applicable customer review is incomplete. Applicant representative Ken asks officer Zoya to open the account immediately and finish the review later. Under the stated local process, this application cannot be approved until the required review is complete. Zoya will provide the specific outstanding-information list through the verified secure channel and arrange a review-status update Thursday at 11:00. Neither complete documents nor that update guarantee approval.

Ken: Applicant representative / Zoya: Account-opening officer

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is missing from A62?

Answer and explanation

A. Requested ownership information

The brief supplies signatory details but leaves the requested ownership information incomplete.

2. What does the local process require before approval here?

Answer and explanation

B. Completion of the required review

The stated process makes review completion a prerequisite for this application, without guaranteeing approval afterward.

3. What does Thursday at 11:00 represent?

Answer and explanation

B. A review-status update

Zoya promises a status update, not account approval or verification of missing information.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

An authorized signatory can operate an account under its mandate without necessarily being its beneficial owner. Supplying signatory details does not automatically answer the ownership question requested in this application.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
Know Your Customer (KYC)
Processes used to understand and verify relevant customer information.complete KYC checks
customer due diligence (CDD)
Assessment of customer identity, relationship purpose, and relevant risk under applicable requirements.conduct customer due diligence
ownership structure
The arrangement of ownership interests in an entity.document the ownership structure
legal entity
An organization recognized as a distinct person under applicable law.identify the legal entity
beneficial owner
An individual meeting the applicable ownership or control definition for the review.identify the beneficial owner
authorized signatory
A person permitted to act or sign for an account under its mandate.verify the authorized signatory
Customer Identification Program (CIP)
A U.S. bank program for obtaining and verifying required customer identity information.follow the Customer Identification Program
control person
An individual meeting the applicable responsibility or control criterion.identify the control person
account mandate
Instructions defining who may operate an account and under what authority.review the account mandate
identity verification
Checking identity using the applicable evidence and process.complete identity verification
documentary verification
Verification using specified documents.perform documentary verification
non-documentary verification
Verification using appropriate methods other than specified identity documents.assess non-documentary verification
nature and purpose
The intended role and use of the customer relationship.establish the nature and purpose
expected activity
The anticipated account use relevant to understanding the relationship.describe expected activity
customer risk profile
The assessed characteristics relevant to the customer's risk context.maintain the customer risk profile
source of funds
The origin of money involved in a relationship or transaction.clarify the source of funds
source of wealth
The origin of a person's or entity's overall accumulated wealth.distinguish source of wealth
onboarding
The process of establishing a new customer relationship.coordinate customer onboarding
account application
A request to establish an account with supplied supporting information.review the account application
outstanding requirement
A requested or applicable condition that has not yet been satisfied.identify an outstanding requirement
verification status
The current stage of checking relevant information.report verification status
risk-based procedure
A process calibrated to relevant assessed risks and applicable requirements.apply a risk-based procedure
exception review
Authorized assessment of a proposed departure from a stated process.request an exception review
approval authority
The defined power to approve a specified action or relationship.confirm approval authority

Requirements depend on the entity, relationship, applicable rules, and risk-based procedures. Current U.S. relief can affect repeated beneficial-owner identification at additional account openings. This first-account case does not teach a universal repeat-collection rule.

C Notice the language

Explain the requirement without making a universal legal claim

A customer may see repeated information requests as unnecessary. Describe the actual gap in this application and the authorized review route. Do not claim every entity or every additional account has identical requirements, and do not confuse signing authority with ownership.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the application
We are reviewing business-account application A62.
Name the supplied information
We have the authorized-signatory details.
Name the gap
The requested ownership information remains incomplete.
Separate roles
Signing authority and beneficial ownership are different questions.
State the local condition
This application requires the review to be complete before approval.
Recognize urgency
I understand that you want to begin using the account promptly.
Avoid an override promise
I cannot bypass the required review.
Make the request specific
I will provide the outstanding-information list.
Protect the submission
Use the verified secure channel for the requested records.
Avoid unnecessary repetition
We will identify what remains missing rather than request everything again.
Explain the context
Requirements must be assessed for this customer and application.
Keep stages distinct
Complete documents do not automatically mean approved onboarding.
Check authority
Any exception requires the applicable authorized review.
Give the next contact
We will update the review status Thursday at 11:00.
Limit the commitment
That is an update appointment, not an approval guarantee.
Close constructively
Send the specified information so the relevant review can continue.
6 language and precision notes
For this application
Restricts a statement to the supplied customer and process.
Authorized to sign
Does not necessarily mean an owner of the entity.
Remains incomplete
Describes information status without accusing the customer.
Before approval
Names a prerequisite, not a guarantee that satisfying it ensures acceptance.
Risk-based
Requires relevant assessment, not arbitrary treatment or a universal checklist.
Verified channel
Protects information without inventing an email address or collection route.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which explanation is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. We have signatory details, but the requested ownership information is incomplete.

The accurate statement distinguishes the information supplied from the separate ownership gap without inventing universal rules.

2. What can Zoya promise?

Answer and explanation

B. A specific missing-information list and a Thursday status update

Zoya can provide the stated communication steps, but the brief gives no authority to guarantee approval or bypass review.

3. Which statement improperly generalizes the case?

Answer and explanation

D. Every additional account always requires identical new ownership collection.

The case does not establish a universal repeat-collection rule, and current U.S. relief can affect additional-account requirements.

4. What does a completed information package establish by itself?

Answer and explanation

D. Information is supplied for review, not automatic approval.

Submission, verification, and approval are distinct stages, so completed paperwork alone does not establish acceptance.

E Conversations

Explain the missing ownership information without promising approval

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Ken: Applicant representative / Zoya: Account-opening officer

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • nature and purpose
  • approval authority
  • exception review
  • authorized signatory
  • verification status
  • outstanding requirement
  • customer due diligence
  • risk-based procedure
  • ownership structure
  • account application
  1. Ken
    We supplied the people authorized to sign. Why is A62 still pending? We need to start using the business account this week.
  2. Zoya
    The requested 1 information is incomplete. We have the signatory details, but those answer a different question. I will identify the precise outstanding items rather than ask you to resend the entire application.
  3. Ken
    Our director can sign for the company. Does identifying her also settle the ownership question?
  4. Zoya
    An 2 is not necessarily a beneficial owner. We need the information relevant to each role under this application's review. One person's signing authority does not establish the full ownership position.
  5. Ken
    Could you open the account now? We can promise to send the remaining information later.
  6. Zoya
    Under this local process, the required 3 review must be complete before this application can be approved. I cannot replace that step with a promise that the information will arrive later.
  7. Ken
    Please send a specific list. Our records team needs to know which ownership details are missing and which documents you already hold.
  8. Zoya
    I will send the 4 list through the verified secure channel. It will distinguish what we hold from what remains needed. That should help your team provide the relevant records without unnecessary duplication.
  9. Ken
    Are these requirements identical for every company and every account? A colleague said the rules had changed for some account openings.
  10. Zoya
    They depend on the applicable rules and 5. Current requirements and relief must be assessed in context. This is your first business-account application here; I should not generalize its requirements to every additional account or entity.
  11. Ken
    The upload screen says complete. Does that mean submitted, verified, or approved? Those would mean different things to my team.
  12. Zoya
    The 6 remains separate from document receipt. Supplying information allows the review to continue. It does not establish that every item has been verified or that the application has been approved.
  13. Ken
    Uploading does not mean the account is ready. Who can review a possible exception?
  14. Zoya
    Any 7 must follow the applicable authorized process. I cannot approve a bypass in this conversation. If a relevant question needs escalation, I will route it rather than invent an exception.
  15. Ken
    The form also asks how we expect to use the account. That is separate from identifying who owns or signs for the company.
  16. Zoya
    Yes. The 8 of the relationship helps establish the relevant customer context. Provide the requested factual description; do not choose an answer merely because you think it will make the review faster.
  17. Ken
    When will I hear from you? An accurate pending status would still help our planning.
  18. Zoya
    We will update the 9 status Thursday at eleven hundred. That is a communication appointment, not an approval guarantee. We will identify any continuing information gap or review step rather than imply a decision we do not have.
  19. Ken
    I will use the verified channel, supply the specified ownership records, and keep the distinction between submission and approval clear internally.
  20. Zoya
    Thank you. The eventual decision remains with the relevant 10. We can make the process understandable and keep the next contact reliable while preserving the required review and the actual account status.

Answers and explanations
  1. ownership structure Turn 2. Ownership structure identifies ownership interests; permission to sign does not establish those interests.
  2. authorized signatory Turn 4. A signatory can act under the mandate without necessarily meeting the applicable ownership definition.
  3. customer due diligence Turn 6. The stated local process requires this customer review before approval; the conversation cannot bypass it.
  4. outstanding requirement Turn 8. The outstanding requirement identifies a specific gap, allowing a focused request instead of unnecessary duplication.
  5. risk-based procedure Turn 10. A risk-based procedure depends on relevant customer circumstances and requirements, not identical rules for every account.
  6. verification status Turn 12. Verification status describes the checks; receiving documents does not establish their verification or approval.
  7. exception review Turn 14. An exception needs authorized review; urgency or a frontline discussion does not approve a departure.
  8. nature and purpose Turn 16. Nature and purpose describes intended account use, distinct from the customer's ownership and signing authority.
  9. account application Turn 18. An account application is a request; a status update does not establish that it is approved.
  10. approval authority Turn 20. Approval authority identifies the permitted decision-maker; completed paperwork does not replace that person's decision.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Ken: We supplied the people authorized to sign. Why is A62 still pending? We need to start using the business account this week.
  2. Zoya: The requested ownership structure information is incomplete. We have the signatory details, but those answer a different question. I will identify the precise outstanding items rather than ask you to resend the entire application.
  3. Ken: Our director can sign for the company. Does identifying her also settle the ownership question?
  4. Zoya: An authorized signatory is not necessarily a beneficial owner. We need the information relevant to each role under this application's review. One person's signing authority does not establish the full ownership position.
  5. Ken: Could you open the account now? We can promise to send the remaining information later.
  6. Zoya: Under this local process, the required customer due diligence review must be complete before this application can be approved. I cannot replace that step with a promise that the information will arrive later.
  7. Ken: Please send a specific list. Our records team needs to know which ownership details are missing and which documents you already hold.
  8. Zoya: I will send the outstanding requirement list through the verified secure channel. It will distinguish what we hold from what remains needed. That should help your team provide the relevant records without unnecessary duplication.
  9. Ken: Are these requirements identical for every company and every account? A colleague said the rules had changed for some account openings.
  10. Zoya: They depend on the applicable rules and risk-based procedure. Current requirements and relief must be assessed in context. This is your first business-account application here; I should not generalize its requirements to every additional account or entity.
  11. Ken: The upload screen says complete. Does that mean submitted, verified, or approved? Those would mean different things to my team.
  12. Zoya: The verification status remains separate from document receipt. Supplying information allows the review to continue. It does not establish that every item has been verified or that the application has been approved.
  13. Ken: Uploading does not mean the account is ready. Who can review a possible exception?
  14. Zoya: Any exception review must follow the applicable authorized process. I cannot approve a bypass in this conversation. If a relevant question needs escalation, I will route it rather than invent an exception.
  15. Ken: The form also asks how we expect to use the account. That is separate from identifying who owns or signs for the company.
  16. Zoya: Yes. The nature and purpose of the relationship helps establish the relevant customer context. Provide the requested factual description; do not choose an answer merely because you think it will make the review faster.
  17. Ken: When will I hear from you? An accurate pending status would still help our planning.
  18. Zoya: We will update the account application status Thursday at eleven hundred. That is a communication appointment, not an approval guarantee. We will identify any continuing information gap or review step rather than imply a decision we do not have.
  19. Ken: I will use the verified channel, supply the specified ownership records, and keep the distinction between submission and approval clear internally.
  20. Zoya: Thank you. The eventual decision remains with the relevant approval authority. We can make the process understandable and keep the next contact reliable while preserving the required review and the actual account status.
Dialogue in the book, page 10 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Ownership information is still required for review

Fictional account-opening exercise: this application's existing request asks for Mira Chen's ownership percentage, which is missing. The application review is incomplete. This case-specific request is not a universal banking requirement; actual decisions follow current authorized procedures.

  1. Account-opening specialist: Could you provide the requested ownership information through our approved channel so the application review can continue?
  2. Company finance officer: Can you open the account first? Our team needs it before the review is finished.
  3. Account-opening specialist: I can't treat the incomplete review as approved. I'll explain the outstanding request clearly.
  4. Company finance officer: Which information is missing? I don't want to resend unrelated documents or guess your requirements.
  5. Account-opening specialist: Please provide Mira Chen's ownership percentage, as requested for this application. When can you supply it?
  6. Company finance officer: Does beneficial owner simply mean the person who signed the application for the company?
  7. Account-opening specialist: Don't assume they're identical. The applicable definition and requested details need the authorized KYC review.
  8. Company finance officer: I'll ask our authorized company contact to provide the specified information and confirm availability.
  9. Account-opening specialist: Thank you. That supports know-your-customer review without promising approval or assigning a risk rating here.
  10. Company finance officer: I'll explain that providing the missing information advances review, but doesn't guarantee account opening.
Identity evidence does not settle ownership

Separate fictional onboarding case: an applicant's identity document is received, while requested company ownership information remains outstanding.

  1. Relationship coordinator: We received the signer's identity document. Can we mark all account-opening checks complete?
  2. Onboarding reviewer: No. The requested ownership information is still outstanding, so the overall review remains open.
  3. Relationship coordinator: Could you identify the next request clearly so I don't ask the client for everything again?
  4. Onboarding reviewer: Please ask for the outstanding ownership information specified in the current application request.
  5. Relationship coordinator: Does completing an identity check under the customer identification program, CIP, establish beneficial ownership too?
  6. Onboarding reviewer: We shouldn't equate those checks. Follow the applicable review requirements for each outstanding item.
  7. Relationship coordinator: I'll keep the received identity document separate from the missing company information in our status update.
  8. Onboarding reviewer: Good. Use the approved channel and avoid circulating identification material in general internal messages.
  9. Relationship coordinator: I'll request an achievable delivery point from the client rather than promise account activation today.
  10. Onboarding reviewer: That gives a clear action and purpose without bypassing review or overstating what one document establishes.
A changed ownership chart needs a verified version

Separate fictional application case: two company ownership charts differ; neither is identified as current, and no onboarding decision is available.

  1. Onboarding analyst: Could you confirm which ownership chart is current? The two submitted versions show different information.
  2. Corporate relationship manager: I can ask the authorized company contact. Should I simply use the one received most recently?
  3. Onboarding analyst: Receipt order alone doesn't establish current ownership. We need a verified explanation of the difference.
  4. Corporate relationship manager: I'll request the current chart and the relevant clarification through the approved document channel.
  5. Onboarding analyst: Please identify both versions so the client can address the actual discrepancy instead of resending blindly.
  6. Corporate relationship manager: Does the mismatch mean we should assign the highest risk rating before the review?
  7. Onboarding analyst: That determination belongs to the authorized process. Here we should describe the discrepancy factually.
  8. Corporate relationship manager: I'll avoid labeling it deception or promising acceptance after a corrected chart arrives.
  9. Onboarding analyst: Good. Ask when the verified information is available, keeping any formal requirements unchanged.
  10. Corporate relationship manager: I'll return the client's confirmed status and preserve the unresolved ownership question until reviewed.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Explain the missing ownership information without promising approval
  1. Read turns 1-10, distinguishing signatory details from ownership information.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; separate document receipt, verification, and approval.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Clarify another account application

Application B14 has signatory details but lacks the requested ownership chart. Hana will coordinate the review. The next status update is Monday at 10:00; approval is not guaranteed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Officer: "The application reference is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. B14

B14 is the specific account-application identifier supplied in this case.

2. Applicant: "The missing document is the ___."

Answer and explanation

D. ownership chart

The brief identifies the ownership chart as missing while signatory details are already supplied.

3. Officer: "The review coordinator is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. Hana

Hana is explicitly responsible for coordinating the stated review.

4. Applicant: "The next status update is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. Monday at 10:00

Monday at ten is an update appointment, not a guaranteed approval time.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

REFERENCE
Course: Banking Operations English
Professional audience: bank operations staff, branch managers, loan operations teams, KYC/AML analysts, fraud operations staff, payment operations teams, compliance staff, and banking leaders
Scope: All banks, customers, accounts, transactions, figures, and local procedures are fictional. This book teaches professional English, not banking, legal, financial, compliance, or investigation advice. Actual obligations depend on the product, jurisdiction, facts, and current requirements. No exercise authorizes an account opening, fund transfer, record alteration, or disclosure of confidential reporting information.
Lesson 01: Account Opening and KYC
Original fictional case: Application A62 is the first business-account application by Northbank Design at this fictional bank. Its authorized-signatory details are supplied, but the ownership information requested for the applicable customer review is incomplete. Applicant representative Ken asks officer Zoya to open the account immediately and finish the review later. Under the stated local process, this application cannot be approved until the required review is complete. Zoya will provide the specific outstanding-information list through the verified secure channel and arrange a review-status update Thursday at 11:00. Neither complete documents nor that update guarantee approval.
Language workshop: Explain the missing ownership information without promising approval
Communication goal: Clarify an account-opening requirement, distinguish ownership from signing authority, and give a useful next step.
Language guidance: An authorized signatory can operate an account under its mandate without necessarily being its beneficial owner. Supplying signatory details does not automatically answer the ownership question requested in this application.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are reviewing business-account application A62. / We have the authorized-signatory details. / The requested ownership information remains incomplete. / Signing authority and beneficial ownership are different questions. / This application requires the review to be complete before approval. / I understand that you want to begin using the account promptly. / I cannot bypass the required review. / I will provide the outstanding-information list. / Use the verified secure channel for the requested records. / We will identify what remains missing rather than request everything again. / Requirements must be assessed for this customer and application. / Complete documents do not automatically mean approved onboarding. / Any exception requires the applicable authorized review. / We will update the review status Thursday at 11:00. / That is an update appointment, not an approval guarantee. / Send the specified information so the relevant review can continue.
Vocabulary: Know Your Customer (KYC): Processes used to understand and verify relevant customer information. / customer due diligence (CDD): Assessment of customer identity, relationship purpose, and relevant risk under applicable requirements. / ownership structure: The arrangement of ownership interests in an entity. / legal entity: An organization recognized as a distinct person under applicable law. / beneficial owner: An individual meeting the applicable ownership or control definition for the review. / authorized signatory: A person permitted to act or sign for an account under its mandate. / Customer Identification Program (CIP): A U.S. bank program for obtaining and verifying required customer identity information. / control person: An individual meeting the applicable responsibility or control criterion. / account mandate: Instructions defining who may operate an account and under what authority. / identity verification: Checking identity using the applicable evidence and process. / documentary verification: Verification using specified documents. / non-documentary verification: Verification using appropriate methods other than specified identity documents. / nature and purpose: The intended role and use of the customer relationship. / expected activity: The anticipated account use relevant to understanding the relationship. / customer risk profile: The assessed characteristics relevant to the customer's risk context. / source of funds: The origin of money involved in a relationship or transaction. / source of wealth: The origin of a person's or entity's overall accumulated wealth. / onboarding: The process of establishing a new customer relationship. / account application: A request to establish an account with supplied supporting information. / outstanding requirement: A requested or applicable condition that has not yet been satisfied. / verification status: The current stage of checking relevant information. / risk-based procedure: A process calibrated to relevant assessed risks and applicable requirements. / exception review: Authorized assessment of a proposed departure from a stated process. / approval authority: The defined power to approve a specified action or relationship.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10, distinguishing signatory details from ownership information. Swap roles for turns 11-20; separate document receipt, verification, and approval. Complete the B14 transfer and check the reference, missing document, owner, and update time.
Partner: Ken: Applicant representative / Zoya: Account-opening officer
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Application B14 has signatory details but lacks the requested ownership chart. Hana will coordinate the review. The next status update is Monday at 10:00; approval is not guaranteed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ken: When will I hear from you? An accurate pending status would still help our planning.
Zoya: We will update the account application status Thursday at eleven hundred. That is a communication appointment, not an approval guarantee. We will identify any continuing information gap or review step rather than imply a decision we do not have.

END REFERENCE
Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

REFERENCE
Course: Banking Operations English
Professional audience: bank operations staff, branch managers, loan operations teams, KYC/AML analysts, fraud operations staff, payment operations teams, compliance staff, and banking leaders
Scope: All banks, customers, accounts, transactions, figures, and local procedures are fictional. This book teaches professional English, not banking, legal, financial, compliance, or investigation advice. Actual obligations depend on the product, jurisdiction, facts, and current requirements. No exercise authorizes an account opening, fund transfer, record alteration, or disclosure of confidential reporting information.
Lesson 01: Account Opening and KYC
Original fictional case: Application A62 is the first business-account application by Northbank Design at this fictional bank. Its authorized-signatory details are supplied, but the ownership information requested for the applicable customer review is incomplete. Applicant representative Ken asks officer Zoya to open the account immediately and finish the review later. Under the stated local process, this application cannot be approved until the required review is complete. Zoya will provide the specific outstanding-information list through the verified secure channel and arrange a review-status update Thursday at 11:00. Neither complete documents nor that update guarantee approval.
Language workshop: Explain the missing ownership information without promising approval
Communication goal: Clarify an account-opening requirement, distinguish ownership from signing authority, and give a useful next step.
Language guidance: An authorized signatory can operate an account under its mandate without necessarily being its beneficial owner. Supplying signatory details does not automatically answer the ownership question requested in this application.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are reviewing business-account application A62. / We have the authorized-signatory details. / The requested ownership information remains incomplete. / Signing authority and beneficial ownership are different questions. / This application requires the review to be complete before approval. / I understand that you want to begin using the account promptly. / I cannot bypass the required review. / I will provide the outstanding-information list. / Use the verified secure channel for the requested records. / We will identify what remains missing rather than request everything again. / Requirements must be assessed for this customer and application. / Complete documents do not automatically mean approved onboarding. / Any exception requires the applicable authorized review. / We will update the review status Thursday at 11:00. / That is an update appointment, not an approval guarantee. / Send the specified information so the relevant review can continue.
Vocabulary: Know Your Customer (KYC): Processes used to understand and verify relevant customer information. / customer due diligence (CDD): Assessment of customer identity, relationship purpose, and relevant risk under applicable requirements. / ownership structure: The arrangement of ownership interests in an entity. / legal entity: An organization recognized as a distinct person under applicable law. / beneficial owner: An individual meeting the applicable ownership or control definition for the review. / authorized signatory: A person permitted to act or sign for an account under its mandate. / Customer Identification Program (CIP): A U.S. bank program for obtaining and verifying required customer identity information. / control person: An individual meeting the applicable responsibility or control criterion. / account mandate: Instructions defining who may operate an account and under what authority. / identity verification: Checking identity using the applicable evidence and process. / documentary verification: Verification using specified documents. / non-documentary verification: Verification using appropriate methods other than specified identity documents. / nature and purpose: The intended role and use of the customer relationship. / expected activity: The anticipated account use relevant to understanding the relationship. / customer risk profile: The assessed characteristics relevant to the customer's risk context. / source of funds: The origin of money involved in a relationship or transaction. / source of wealth: The origin of a person's or entity's overall accumulated wealth. / onboarding: The process of establishing a new customer relationship. / account application: A request to establish an account with supplied supporting information. / outstanding requirement: A requested or applicable condition that has not yet been satisfied. / verification status: The current stage of checking relevant information. / risk-based procedure: A process calibrated to relevant assessed risks and applicable requirements. / exception review: Authorized assessment of a proposed departure from a stated process. / approval authority: The defined power to approve a specified action or relationship.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10, distinguishing signatory details from ownership information. Swap roles for turns 11-20; separate document receipt, verification, and approval. Complete the B14 transfer and check the reference, missing document, owner, and update time.
Partner: Ken: Applicant representative / Zoya: Account-opening officer
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Application B14 has signatory details but lacks the requested ownership chart. Hana will coordinate the review. The next status update is Monday at 10:00; approval is not guaranteed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ken: When will I hear from you? An accurate pending status would still help our planning.
Zoya: We will update the account application status Thursday at eleven hundred. That is a communication appointment, not an approval guarantee. We will identify any continuing information gap or review step rather than imply a decision we do not have.
Ken: I will use the verified channel, supply the specified ownership records, and keep the distinction between submission and approval clear internally.
Zoya: Thank you. The eventual decision remains with the relevant approval authority. We can make the process understandable and keep the next contact reliable while preserving the required review and the actual account status.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. 02AML Monitoring and Suspicious ActivityAn unusual-activity alert is a question, not a verdict+

Your goal

Write a neutral internal handoff and preserve confidential reporting boundaries.

This lesson in the book, page 13 ↗

A Read the situation

An unusual-activity alert is a question, not a verdict

Monitoring analyst Sam receives alert T82 because recent transfers differ from the activity recorded in a business customer profile. The transaction records are available, but the business purpose and explanation for the change have not been established. A draft note says the customer is laundering money. Reviewer Aisha asks Sam to replace that conclusion with the observed difference and open questions, then follow the authorized Anti-Money Laundering (AML) review route. No reporting decision is supplied in this case. Suspicious Activity Report (SAR) information and its existence or nonexistence must not be disclosed through unauthorized channels.

Sam: Monitoring analyst / Aisha: AML reviewer

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does T82 identify?

Answer and explanation

B. Activity differing from the recorded customer profile

The alert identifies a difference requiring review, while its explanation remains unestablished.

2. What is wrong with the draft note?

Answer and explanation

C. It states wrongdoing as established without the supporting finding.

The supplied facts do not establish money laundering, so the accusatory conclusion overstates the evidence.

3. What reporting decision is supplied?

Answer and explanation

A. None

The case deliberately provides no reporting decision, and the analyst must not invent one.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

An alert is not proof of criminal conduct and does not itself establish a reporting decision. A review can preserve concern without asserting wrongdoing or assuming that the activity has an innocent explanation.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
Anti-Money Laundering (AML)
Measures addressing the misuse of financial services to conceal or facilitate illicit funds.conduct an AML review
Bank Secrecy Act (BSA)
A U.S. framework of financial recordkeeping and reporting requirements.assess applicable BSA requirements
transaction monitoring
Review of financial activity for patterns requiring attention under the applicable process.perform transaction monitoring
monitoring alert
A signal identifying activity for further review.investigate a monitoring alert
customer profile
Recorded information about a customer and the relevant relationship context.compare the customer profile
observed activity
Transactions or conduct actually recorded or identified.describe observed activity
business purpose
The commercial reason given or established for activity.clarify the business purpose
unusual activity
Activity differing from an expected or relevant pattern and requiring context.assess unusual activity
suspicious activity
Activity meeting the relevant concern or reporting assessment under applicable requirements.evaluate suspicious activity
Suspicious Activity Report (SAR)
A confidential report made under applicable suspicious-activity reporting requirements.protect SAR confidentiality
alert disposition
The recorded outcome of an alert review.document the alert disposition
case escalation
Referral of a matter to the appropriate review level.coordinate case escalation
supporting records
Documents or data relevant to assessing the observed activity.preserve supporting records
transaction narrative
A clear description of relevant transaction facts and context.prepare a transaction narrative
source attribution
Identification of where information came from.retain source attribution
risk indicator
Information potentially relevant to assessing a concern.evaluate a risk indicator
contextual information
Background needed to interpret the observed facts.obtain contextual information
corroborating evidence
Additional information supporting or challenging an interpretation.review corroborating evidence
review rationale
The documented reasoning for an assessment or next step.explain the review rationale
reporting decision
A determination under the applicable process about required reporting.distinguish the reporting decision
confidentiality restriction
A limit on access or disclosure of protected information.observe confidentiality restrictions
authorized channel
An approved route for transmitting relevant information.use an authorized channel
need-to-know basis
Access limited to those requiring information for an authorized role.share on a need-to-know basis
neutral description
Factual wording without an unsupported adverse or favorable conclusion.use a neutral description

SAR confidentiality includes information revealing whether a SAR exists. Do not disclose filing or nonfiling status to customers or unauthorized recipients. Use the applicable authorized channels and current requirements; this exercise teaches no filing threshold.

C Notice the language

Separate a system signal from a human conclusion

An alert can feel authoritative because a system produced it. Explain the actual observation and the missing context without treating the signal as proof. Keep the handoff factual and avoid using customer nationality, accent, or unfamiliarity as a substitute for relevant evidence.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the item
We are reviewing monitoring alert T82.
Describe the observation
The transfers differ from the activity recorded in the customer profile.
Name the missing context
The business purpose has not been established.
Remove the unsupported finding
Do not describe money laundering as proven.
Preserve the concern
The difference still requires review.
Avoid premature reassurance
We have not established a harmless explanation either.
Link the evidence
Attach the relevant transaction references through the authorized channel.
Attribute the source
Separate system records from unverified explanations.
Keep the note neutral
Describe the observed activity and open questions.
Use the review route
Escalate through the applicable AML process.
Separate reporting
An alert review is not itself a reporting decision.
Protect confidentiality
Do not disclose SAR information through unauthorized channels.
Restrict distribution
Share the handoff only on a need-to-know basis.
Avoid an irrelevant proxy
An accent or nationality is not evidence of wrongdoing.
Record the rationale
Explain why the matter needs further context.
Close the handoff
Keep facts, uncertainty, review action, and reporting status distinct.
6 language and precision notes
Differs from
Reports a comparison without supplying its cause.
Has not been established
Preserves an unanswered question.
Proven
Requires evidence beyond the existence of an alert.
Neither conclusion
Avoids both unsupported accusation and unsupported clearance.
Whether a report exists
Information that can itself be confidential.
Relevant evidence
Refers to the activity and context, not a demographic shortcut.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which internal note fits the case?

Answer and explanation

A. T82 identifies transfers differing from the profile; business purpose unestablished; referred for authorized review.

The appropriate note describes the observed difference, missing context, and review action without inventing a finding or report.

2. What should the reviewer not use as proof?

Answer and explanation

C. The customer's accent or nationality

Accent or nationality is not evidence that the observed transactions constitute wrongdoing.

3. Which disclosure is inappropriate through an unauthorized channel?

Answer and explanation

D. Whether a SAR exists for the customer

Information revealing SAR existence or nonexistence is confidential and must not be disclosed through unauthorized channels.

4. What should the record preserve?

Answer and explanation

D. Sources, observed facts, uncertainty, and the review rationale

The complete record links evidence to the review question instead of substituting a score, partial summary, or assumption.

Check explanations in the book, page 87 ↗

An unusual-activity alert is a question, not a verdict

Sam: Monitoring analyst / Aisha: AML reviewer

  8. Aisha
    Maintain 4 for each item. Separate recorded transactions, customer information, and unverified explanations. Otherwise, a later reader may mistake an assertion or assumption for a fact established by the original records.

  13. Sam

Answers and explanations
  1. neutral description Turn 2. A neutral description records the observed difference without converting an unresolved concern into a proven offense.
  2. business purpose Turn 4. Business purpose is the relevant explanation for the activity; it remains unestablished rather than necessarily absent or illicit.
  3. unusual activity Turn 6. Unusual activity differs from the relevant pattern and requires evaluation; it is neither automatic wrongdoing nor automatic clearance.
  4. source attribution Turn 8. Source attribution distinguishes records from statements or assumptions, allowing reviewers to assess each item's basis.
  5. reporting decision Turn 10. A reporting decision follows the applicable assessment process; the existence of a monitoring alert does not establish one.
  6. confidentiality restriction Turn 12. The confidentiality restriction protects SAR information, including existence or nonexistence, from unauthorized disclosure.
  7. authorized channel Turn 14. An authorized channel limits information to the permitted review route rather than a broadly visible service note.
  8. review rationale Turn 16. The review rationale explains the evidence-based reason for further assessment without substituting a label for analysis.
  9. case escalation Turn 18. Case escalation routes the concern to the proper review level; it is an action rather than an established adverse finding.
  10. alert disposition Turn 20. Alert disposition records the actual review outcome; it must not be guessed while assessment remains incomplete.

Dialogue in the book, page 19 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

An alert is not a finding of wrongdoing

Fictional internal anti-money-laundering review: a monitoring alert identifies unusual transactions, but their explanation is unknown. No detection thresholds or customer-contact tactics are discussed.

  1. AML analyst: The alert identifies unusual transactions, but the draft note says the customer is laundering money.
  2. Monitoring supervisor: That conclusion is unsupported. Record the observed activity and the context still needed for review.
  3. AML analyst: Can I call the pattern confirmed structuring because the monitoring system generated an alert?
  4. Monitoring supervisor: No. An alert isn't a finding of intent or an established explanation for the activity.
  5. AML analyst: I'll separate the system flag from our assessment and preserve the supporting records internally.
  6. Monitoring supervisor: Good. Any suspicious activity report, or SAR, decision follows the authorized process, not this initial label.
  7. AML analyst: Should I tell the relationship team to discuss the alert's details directly with the customer?
  8. Monitoring supervisor: Don't initiate disclosure outside the approved process. Escalate communication questions through authorized channels, including tipping-off concerns.
  9. AML analyst: I'll keep the note neutral and ask the reviewer which context is missing for the assessment.
  10. Monitoring supervisor: That supports a careful review without prejudging the customer or disclosing restricted internal monitoring information.
A familiar explanation is still unverified

Separate fictional internal review: a colleague suggests an unusual transfer pattern reflects seasonal business activity, but the case evidence has not established that explanation.

  1. Monitoring analyst: A colleague says these transfers probably reflect seasonal business activity. Can we close the alert?
  2. AML review lead: That is a possible explanation, not a verified finding in the evidence we currently have.
  3. Monitoring analyst: Then the neutral note should preserve the hypothesis without presenting it as confirmed context.
  4. AML review lead: Yes. Use the authorized review process to identify what evidence is relevant and available.
  5. Monitoring analyst: Would replacing the suspicion with a reassuring explanation be fairer to the customer?
  6. AML review lead: Fairness requires accurate evidence, not either an accusation or an unsupported reassuring conclusion.
  7. Monitoring analyst: I'll state that the explanation remains unverified and keep the observed activity separate.
  8. AML review lead: Good. Don't include operational monitoring details in broad messages or make independent disclosure decisions.
  9. Monitoring analyst: I'll route any communication and reporting questions to the authorized reviewer without assuming a SAR outcome.
  10. AML review lead: Exactly. The next step is a documented assessment, not closing or escalating solely on a colleague's guess.
An incomplete record limits the comparison

Separate fictional monitoring case: an analyst compares current transactions with an earlier period, but the earlier-period extract is incomplete.

  1. Financial crime analyst: The current activity looks higher than the earlier period, but the older extract is incomplete.
  2. Monitoring quality reviewer: Then we can't treat that comparison as a verified change across two equivalent periods.
  3. Financial crime analyst: Should the internal note say the increase may reflect missing historical records rather than customer behavior?
  4. Monitoring quality reviewer: State the data limitation without choosing a cause. Both the comparison and explanation need review.
  5. Financial crime analyst: I'll request the authorized complete extract and avoid treating missing transactions as zero activity.
  6. Monitoring quality reviewer: Good. Preserve the current alert evidence while identifying the specific gap in the historical comparison.
  7. Financial crime analyst: Does the data gap itself establish deliberate concealment or justify calling the activity structuring?
  8. Monitoring quality reviewer: No. It doesn't establish intent, and those labels shouldn't substitute for the authorized AML assessment.
  9. Financial crime analyst: I'll keep the case note restricted, neutral, and explicit about the incomplete comparison.
  10. Monitoring quality reviewer: That gives the next reviewer a sound starting point without an unsupported allegation or premature closure.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

An unusual-activity alert is a question, not a verdict
  1. Read turns 1-10; replace the accusation with the observed profile difference.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20, maintaining the confidential-reporting boundary.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Complete a neutral alert handoff

Alert U19 identifies activity differing from a recorded profile. Its business purpose is unconfirmed. Noor owns the authorized review. No reporting decision is supplied.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Analyst: "The alert reference is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. U19

U19 is the specific monitoring-alert identifier supplied in the case.

2. Reviewer: "The business purpose remains ___."

Answer and explanation

B. unconfirmed

The brief does not establish the explanation for the changed activity.

3. Analyst: "The assigned reviewer is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. Noor

Noor is explicitly responsible for the authorized review.

4. Reviewer: "This alert alone does not establish a reporting ___."

Answer and explanation

D. decision

An alert does not establish a reporting decision, and none is supplied here.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Lesson 02: AML Monitoring and Suspicious Activity
Original fictional case: Monitoring analyst Sam receives alert T82 because recent transfers differ from the activity recorded in a business customer profile. The transaction records are available, but the business purpose and explanation for the change have not been established. A draft note says the customer is laundering money. Reviewer Aisha asks Sam to replace that conclusion with the observed difference and open questions, then follow the authorized Anti-Money Laundering (AML) review route. No reporting decision is supplied in this case. Suspicious Activity Report (SAR) information and its existence or nonexistence must not be disclosed through unauthorized channels.
Language workshop: An unusual-activity alert is a question, not a verdict
Communication goal: Write a neutral internal handoff and preserve confidential reporting boundaries.
Language guidance: An alert is not proof of criminal conduct and does not itself establish a reporting decision. A review can preserve concern without asserting wrongdoing or assuming that the activity has an innocent explanation.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are reviewing monitoring alert T82. / The transfers differ from the activity recorded in the customer profile. / The business purpose has not been established. / Do not describe money laundering as proven. / The difference still requires review. / We have not established a harmless explanation either. / Attach the relevant transaction references through the authorized channel. / Separate system records from unverified explanations. / Describe the observed activity and open questions. / Escalate through the applicable AML process. / An alert review is not itself a reporting decision. / Do not disclose SAR information through unauthorized channels. / Share the handoff only on a need-to-know basis. / An accent or nationality is not evidence of wrongdoing. / Explain why the matter needs further context. / Keep facts, uncertainty, review action, and reporting status distinct.
Vocabulary: Anti-Money Laundering (AML): Measures addressing the misuse of financial services to conceal or facilitate illicit funds. / Bank Secrecy Act (BSA): A U.S. framework of financial recordkeeping and reporting requirements. / transaction monitoring: Review of financial activity for patterns requiring attention under the applicable process. / monitoring alert: A signal identifying activity for further review. / customer profile: Recorded information about a customer and the relevant relationship context. / observed activity: Transactions or conduct actually recorded or identified. / business purpose: The commercial reason given or established for activity. / unusual activity: Activity differing from an expected or relevant pattern and requiring context. / suspicious activity: Activity meeting the relevant concern or reporting assessment under applicable requirements. / Suspicious Activity Report (SAR): A confidential report made under applicable suspicious-activity reporting requirements. / alert disposition: The recorded outcome of an alert review. / case escalation: Referral of a matter to the appropriate review level. / supporting records: Documents or data relevant to assessing the observed activity. / transaction narrative: A clear description of relevant transaction facts and context. / source attribution: Identification of where information came from. / risk indicator: Information potentially relevant to assessing a concern. / contextual information: Background needed to interpret the observed facts. / corroborating evidence: Additional information supporting or challenging an interpretation. / review rationale: The documented reasoning for an assessment or next step. / reporting decision: A determination under the applicable process about required reporting. / confidentiality restriction: A limit on access or disclosure of protected information. / authorized channel: An approved route for transmitting relevant information. / need-to-know basis: Access limited to those requiring information for an authorized role. / neutral description: Factual wording without an unsupported adverse or favorable conclusion.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; replace the accusation with the observed profile difference. Swap roles for turns 11-20, maintaining the confidential-reporting boundary. Complete the U19 transfer and check the alert, unknown purpose, reviewer, and decision status.
Partner: Sam: Monitoring analyst / Aisha: AML reviewer

Sam: When the authorized review is complete, its actual result can be recorded with the supporting rationale and the appropriate confidentiality controls.
Aisha: Yes. The alert disposition must reflect the completed assessment, not a guess made to clear the queue. Keep the evidence, review stage, and any protected reporting information distinct throughout the record.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.
