03Loan Operations and DocumentationA checked condition points to the wrong agreement version+

Your goal

Escalate a document mismatch without silently changing the evidence or authorizing a drawdown.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

A checked condition points to the wrong agreement version

Loan package L73 uses approved agreement version 3. The operations checklist marks condition C4 complete, but the attached supporting certificate refers to agreement version 2. The team has not established whether that certificate satisfies C4 for version 3. Operations officer Ravi and documentation reviewer Mae must correct the status and obtain an authorized assessment. Under the stated local process, the affected drawdown is not authorized while this condition remains unresolved. The mismatch alone does not prove that the entire loan is invalid. Neither participant may edit the certificate reference or mark the condition satisfied merely to meet a requested funding date.

Ravi: Loan operations officer / Mae: Documentation reviewer

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which agreement version governs this package?

Answer and explanation

D. Approved version 3

The brief identifies approved version 3; an attachment, upload order, or unassessed similarity cannot change that status.

2. What does the supporting certificate reference?

Answer and explanation

D. Version 2

The certificate references version 2, creating the unresolved mismatch with version 3.

3. What is the affected drawdown status under the stated process?

Answer and explanation

B. Not authorized while C4 remains unresolved

The local process leaves the affected drawdown unauthorized pending resolution of the specific condition.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The certificate mismatch leaves C4 unresolved; it does not automatically invalidate the entire facility. Satisfaction, waiver, and release authorization are different decisions, each requiring its applicable authority and evidence.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
loan facility
An agreed arrangement providing borrowing subject to specified terms.administer a loan facility
loan agreement
The contract establishing the borrowing terms and obligations.review the loan agreement
drawdown
Use or disbursement of funds under a loan facility.authorize a drawdown
condition precedent
A condition that must be satisfied or appropriately waived before a specified action.verify a condition precedent
supporting certificate
A document certifying specified information relevant to a requirement.examine a supporting certificate
approved version
The document issue authorized for the relevant purpose.identify the approved version
document discrepancy
A mismatch between documents or their references.record a document discrepancy
execution copy
The version prepared or used for formal signing.confirm the execution copy
executed document
A document signed or otherwise completed as required for its execution.retain the executed document
amendment
A documented change to an existing agreement.review an amendment
waiver
An authorized relinquishment or modification of a requirement under applicable terms.confirm a waiver
consent
Permission given by the relevant party for a specified action.obtain the required consent
covenant
A contractual undertaking or restriction.monitor a covenant
security document
A document establishing or recording relevant collateral rights.verify the security document
collateral
Property or rights supporting repayment under an agreed arrangement.identify the collateral
guarantor
A party undertaking specified obligations relating to another party's debt.confirm the guarantor
facility agent
A party coordinating specified administrative functions under a facility agreement.contact the facility agent
conditions checklist
A record tracking requirements and their evidence or status.update the conditions checklist
document index
A list linking required documents to their identifiers and locations.maintain the document index
version control
Management of document issues and their applicable status.maintain version control
exception log
A record of unresolved departures or discrepancies.update the exception log
funding request
A request for a loan disbursement under the applicable arrangement.review the funding request
release authorization
Approval allowing a specified disbursement or action to proceed.verify release authorization
document retention
Preservation of records under the relevant requirements.follow document-retention requirements

A requested funding date does not correct a document reference or authorize disbursement. Preserve the original certificate and obtain the required assessment. Do not silently replace version 2 with version 3 in the evidence.

C Notice the language

A precise challenge can protect the working relationship

A checked box can be mistaken for an established fact, especially near a funding deadline. Identify the exact reference mismatch and its consequence for the current task. Challenge the evidence politely without accusing the preparer or claiming a wider legal conclusion.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Name the package
We are reviewing loan package L73.
Identify the approved issue
The package uses agreement version 3.
State the conflicting reference
The certificate refers to version 2.
Locate the affected condition
The mismatch concerns condition C4.
Correct the checklist
C4 should not be marked complete on this evidence alone.
Preserve the document
Do not edit the certificate reference.
Limit the conclusion
This mismatch does not by itself invalidate the whole loan.
Request authorized review
Please assess whether the certificate satisfies C4 for version 3.
Keep funding status explicit
The affected drawdown is not authorized while the condition remains unresolved.
Distinguish a waiver
A request to overlook the issue is not an approved waiver.
Respect the deadline without inventing authority
I understand the requested funding date, but the evidence still needs review.
Link the references
Record the package, condition, and both version identifiers.
Avoid assumed equivalence
Similar wording does not prove that the documents are interchangeable.
Keep the next action clear
Obtain the relevant documentation decision through the authorized route.
Track the exception
Leave the discrepancy visible until its actual resolution is recorded.
Close the handoff
Report the reviewed evidence and authorization status separately.
6 language and precision notes
Refers to
Reports the actual document reference without deciding its legal effect.
Should not be marked complete
Challenges a status unsupported by the supplied evidence.
By itself
Limits the conclusion that follows from one observed mismatch.
Satisfied versus waived
Meeting a condition differs from an authorized decision to waive it.
Requested funding
A desired disbursement time, not evidence of permission.
Separately
Prevents document review from being mistaken for release authorization.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which checklist correction is supported?

Answer and explanation

C. Mark C4 unresolved pending authorized assessment of the version mismatch.

The evidence does not yet establish C4 satisfaction for version 3, so the specific condition requires review.

2. Which statement invents a waiver?

Answer and explanation

D. The client wants funding today, so C4 is waived.

A requested funding date does not supply the authority or decision needed to waive a condition.

3. What should happen to the original certificate?

Answer and explanation

B. Preserve it with its actual reference and obtain the necessary assessment.

The original is evidence and must remain traceable while the mismatch is assessed.

4. What does resolving a document question not automatically establish?

Answer and explanation

B. A separate release authorization unless the applicable process provides it

Document satisfaction and funding authorization are distinct matters under the stated process and must not be conflated.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

A checked condition points to the wrong agreement version

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Ravi: Loan operations officer / Mae: Documentation reviewer

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • version control
  • condition precedent
  • supporting certificate
  • document index
  • document discrepancy
  • drawdown
  • waiver
  • release authorization
  • exception log
  • funding request
  1. Ravi
    C4 is checked complete in L73, but its certificate refers to agreement version 2. The approved package uses version 3.
  2. Mae
    Record the 1. We have not established whether that certificate satisfies C4 for version 3. A completed checkbox does not remove the conflicting reference or supply the missing assessment.
  3. Ravi
    C4 may be unchanged, but I have not checked. Who can assess whether this certificate still satisfies it?
  4. Mae
    That needs authorized review of the 2 and supporting evidence. Similar wording is not enough for us to declare satisfaction. Equally, the mismatch alone does not prove that the whole facility is invalid.
  5. Ravi
    The team wants funding today. Can I change the certificate's version number to match?
  6. Mae
    Preserve the 3 as received. Do not edit its reference to make the checklist look consistent. We need the relevant assessment and any properly issued replacement or other authorized resolution.
  7. Ravi
    I will correct the checklist status and note the exact package and condition. The requested funding date will remain a request, not an authorization.
  8. Mae
    Keep the 4 status explicit. Under this local process, the affected drawdown is not authorized while C4 remains unresolved. A deadline does not establish permission to release the funds.
  9. Ravi
    Someone suggested calling the issue waived because the client agrees to provide another certificate later. That does not sound like an authorized decision.
  10. Mae
    It is not an established 5. Satisfaction and waiver are different concepts, and each needs its proper basis. We cannot convert the client's proposal into an approved waiver merely by changing the label.
  11. Ravi
    I will send the document references to the appropriate reviewer. We should keep both versions available so the question can be assessed accurately.
  12. Mae
    Maintain 6. Identify version 3 as the approved agreement and version 2 as the certificate's reference. That distinction lets the reviewer understand the actual issue instead of guessing which document we meant.
  13. Ravi
    I will flag C4 rather than mark the entire facility defective. We have identified one reference mismatch, not reviewed every document.
  14. Mae
    Use the 7 to identify C4 and the pending assessment. Keep the scope precise. We have a document-reference question affecting a condition, not evidence that every part of the facility is defective.
  15. Ravi
    Once the reviewer decides what is needed, I will link that decision to the evidence. I will not remove the old certificate from the record.
  16. Mae
    Update the 8 so the decision and supporting material are traceable. If a replacement is issued, its identity and status need to be clear without erasing the document that prompted the question.
  17. Ravi
    Would satisfying or appropriately waiving C4 automatically authorize release of the funds?
  18. Mae
    Check the separate 9 requirements. Resolving this condition does not automatically establish every other required approval. Report the document decision and funding status separately so nobody infers permission from a narrower update.
  19. Ravi
    My handoff will show L73, C4, approved version 3, the certificate's version 2 reference, the unresolved status, and the authorized review request.
  20. Mae
    Good. Keep the 10 visible as well, but do not let its urgency replace the evidence. The next team needs the actual condition and authorization status, not a reassuring checkbox unsupported by the record.

Answers and explanations
  1. document discrepancy Turn 2. The document discrepancy is the observed mismatch between the certificate and approved agreement, not a conclusion about the entire loan's validity.
  2. condition precedent Turn 4. The condition precedent is the requirement relevant to the proposed action; its satisfaction must be assessed against the applicable agreement.
  3. supporting certificate Turn 6. The supporting certificate is evidence with an actual reference; silently changing it would conceal the mismatch instead of resolving it.
  4. drawdown Turn 8. Drawdown is the use or disbursement of facility funds; it remains unauthorized under the stated process while C4 is unresolved.
  5. waiver Turn 10. A waiver requires the applicable authority and decision; a client's promise or operational preference does not establish one.
  6. version control Turn 12. Version control preserves which document issue applies and prevents a prior version from being silently treated as the approved one.
  7. exception log Turn 14. The exception log records the specific unresolved discrepancy and its status, not an unsupported conclusion about every loan document.
  8. document index Turn 16. The document index links the condition, relevant records, and decision while preserving the original evidence and its history.
  9. release authorization Turn 18. Release authorization permits the disbursement; resolving one document condition does not automatically supply every remaining approval.
  10. funding request Turn 20. The funding request expresses the desired disbursement; it remains distinct from the evidence review and actual authority to release funds.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Ravi: C4 is checked complete in L73, but its certificate refers to agreement version 2. The approved package uses version 3.
  2. Mae: Record the document discrepancy. We have not established whether that certificate satisfies C4 for version 3. A completed checkbox does not remove the conflicting reference or supply the missing assessment.
  3. Ravi: C4 may be unchanged, but I have not checked. Who can assess whether this certificate still satisfies it?
  4. Mae: That needs authorized review of the condition precedent and supporting evidence. Similar wording is not enough for us to declare satisfaction. Equally, the mismatch alone does not prove that the whole facility is invalid.
  5. Ravi: The team wants funding today. Can I change the certificate's version number to match?
  6. Mae: Preserve the supporting certificate as received. Do not edit its reference to make the checklist look consistent. We need the relevant assessment and any properly issued replacement or other authorized resolution.
  7. Ravi: I will correct the checklist status and note the exact package and condition. The requested funding date will remain a request, not an authorization.
  8. Mae: Keep the drawdown status explicit. Under this local process, the affected drawdown is not authorized while C4 remains unresolved. A deadline does not establish permission to release the funds.
  9. Ravi: Someone suggested calling the issue waived because the client agrees to provide another certificate later. That does not sound like an authorized decision.
  10. Mae: It is not an established waiver. Satisfaction and waiver are different concepts, and each needs its proper basis. We cannot convert the client's proposal into an approved waiver merely by changing the label.
  11. Ravi: I will send the document references to the appropriate reviewer. We should keep both versions available so the question can be assessed accurately.
  12. Mae: Maintain version control. Identify version 3 as the approved agreement and version 2 as the certificate's reference. That distinction lets the reviewer understand the actual issue instead of guessing which document we meant.
  13. Ravi: I will flag C4 rather than mark the entire facility defective. We have identified one reference mismatch, not reviewed every document.
  14. Mae: Use the exception log to identify C4 and the pending assessment. Keep the scope precise. We have a document-reference question affecting a condition, not evidence that every part of the facility is defective.
  15. Ravi: Once the reviewer decides what is needed, I will link that decision to the evidence. I will not remove the old certificate from the record.
  16. Mae: Update the document index so the decision and supporting material are traceable. If a replacement is issued, its identity and status need to be clear without erasing the document that prompted the question.
  17. Ravi: Would satisfying or appropriately waiving C4 automatically authorize release of the funds?
  18. Mae: Check the separate release authorization requirements. Resolving this condition does not automatically establish every other required approval. Report the document decision and funding status separately so nobody infers permission from a narrower update.
  19. Ravi: My handoff will show L73, C4, approved version 3, the certificate's version 2 reference, the unresolved status, and the authorized review request.
  20. Mae: Good. Keep the funding request visible as well, but do not let its urgency replace the evidence. The next team needs the actual condition and authorization status, not a reassuring checkbox unsupported by the record.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Which agreement version supports the completed condition?

Fictional loan-operations review: a condition is marked complete, but its supporting document references a different agreement version; no funding authorization is given.

  1. Loan operations analyst: The checklist marks this condition complete, but the supporting document refers to another agreement version.
  2. Documentation coordinator: Could you identify the two references so we can ask the authorized reviewer to clarify?
  3. Loan operations analyst: The checklist cites version three; the attachment cites version two. Their relationship isn't explained.
  4. Documentation coordinator: Then we need confirmation of the applicable agreement and evidence before relying on that completed status.
  5. Loan operations analyst: Can I assume the condition precedent is unchanged because the document titles are similar?
  6. Documentation coordinator: No. Similar titles don't establish equivalent requirements or satisfaction of a condition.
  7. Loan operations analyst: I'll request the current support and keep the loan-boarding question open for the authorized team.
  8. Documentation coordinator: Please also preserve the existing documents rather than change their version labels to make them match.
  9. Loan operations analyst: Understood. I'll record the discrepancy without deciding that an exception or funding approval has been granted.
  10. Documentation coordinator: Good. This clarification should establish the document basis, not substitute for the required lending review.
Received collateral document or verified collateral record?

Separate fictional loan-boarding case: a collateral document is received, but its asset reference has not been checked against the loan record.

  1. Loan boarding coordinator: The collateral document arrived. Does received mean the collateral check is complete for boarding?
  2. Loan documentation lead: Not here. The asset reference still needs checking against the applicable loan record.
  3. Loan boarding coordinator: Could you clarify which reference the reviewer needs, rather than asking me to resend the entire file?
  4. Loan documentation lead: We need the asset identifier and its documented link to this loan, through the approved process.
  5. Loan boarding coordinator: The borrower name matches. Is that enough to assume the document covers the intended asset?
  6. Loan documentation lead: No. A matching name alone doesn't establish the collateral relationship or legal sufficiency.
  7. Loan boarding coordinator: I'll ask the document owner for clarification and describe the receipt separately from verification.
  8. Loan documentation lead: Good. Leave any condition-precedent or exception decision with the authorized reviewer.
  9. Loan boarding coordinator: I'll keep the check pending and avoid telling the relationship team that boarding is cleared.
  10. Loan documentation lead: That preserves the distinction between having a document and establishing what it supports in this case.
An exception request is not an approved exception

Separate fictional loan-operations case: a record says exception submitted, while a team note shortens it to exception approved; the decision is unavailable.

  1. Operations case officer: The team note says exception approved, but the source record only says exception submitted.
  2. Credit administration liaison: Could you clarify whether any decision record supports the stronger wording in that note?
  3. Operations case officer: None is attached. I can't establish approval from the information currently available to us.
  4. Credit administration liaison: Then request the authorized status and keep submitted distinct from accepted or approved.
  5. Operations case officer: Would it be reasonable to assume approval because the loan-boarding task is scheduled for tomorrow?
  6. Credit administration liaison: No. A schedule doesn't establish that a condition precedent or exception has been authorized.
  7. Operations case officer: I'll correct the coordination status to submitted and ask who can verify the decision.
  8. Credit administration liaison: Please preserve the source record and identify the exact exception reference for the reviewer.
  9. Operations case officer: I'll also tell the receiving team that this clarification does not itself authorize boarding or funding.
  10. Credit administration liaison: Good. We need a confirmed status, not a convenient inference from the planned timetable.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

A checked condition points to the wrong agreement version
  1. Read turns 1-10, naming L73, C4, and the two different version references.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; distinguish satisfaction, waiver, and release authorization.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Identify another document mismatch

Package N26 uses approved agreement version 5. The certificate supporting condition D2 refers to version 4. Satisfaction is unresolved, and the affected drawdown is not authorized under the stated process.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Officer: "The package reference is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. N26

N26 is the package identifier given for this separate documentation case.

2. Reviewer: "The approved agreement version is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 5

Version 5 is the approved agreement, not the older certificate reference.

3. Officer: "The certificate refers to version ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 4

Version 4 is the actual reference on the supporting certificate.

4. Reviewer: "The affected condition is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. D2

D2 is the condition whose satisfaction remains unresolved because of the mismatch.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Lesson 03: Loan Operations and Documentation
Original fictional case: Loan package L73 uses approved agreement version 3. The operations checklist marks condition C4 complete, but the attached supporting certificate refers to agreement version 2. The team has not established whether that certificate satisfies C4 for version 3. Operations officer Ravi and documentation reviewer Mae must correct the status and obtain an authorized assessment. Under the stated local process, the affected drawdown is not authorized while this condition remains unresolved. The mismatch alone does not prove that the entire loan is invalid. Neither participant may edit the certificate reference or mark the condition satisfied merely to meet a requested funding date.
Language workshop: A checked condition points to the wrong agreement version
Communication goal: Escalate a document mismatch without silently changing the evidence or authorizing a drawdown.
Language guidance: The certificate mismatch leaves C4 unresolved; it does not automatically invalidate the entire facility. Satisfaction, waiver, and release authorization are different decisions, each requiring its applicable authority and evidence.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are reviewing loan package L73. / The package uses agreement version 3. / The certificate refers to version 2. / The mismatch concerns condition C4. / C4 should not be marked complete on this evidence alone. / Do not edit the certificate reference. / This mismatch does not by itself invalidate the whole loan. / Please assess whether the certificate satisfies C4 for version 3. / The affected drawdown is not authorized while the condition remains unresolved. / A request to overlook the issue is not an approved waiver. / I understand the requested funding date, but the evidence still needs review. / Record the package, condition, and both version identifiers. / Similar wording does not prove that the documents are interchangeable. / Obtain the relevant documentation decision through the authorized route. / Leave the discrepancy visible until its actual resolution is recorded. / Report the reviewed evidence and authorization status separately.
Vocabulary: loan facility: An agreed arrangement providing borrowing subject to specified terms. / loan agreement: The contract establishing the borrowing terms and obligations. / drawdown: Use or disbursement of funds under a loan facility. / condition precedent: A condition that must be satisfied or appropriately waived before a specified action. / supporting certificate: A document certifying specified information relevant to a requirement. / approved version: The document issue authorized for the relevant purpose. / document discrepancy: A mismatch between documents or their references. / execution copy: The version prepared or used for formal signing. / executed document: A document signed or otherwise completed as required for its execution. / amendment: A documented change to an existing agreement. / waiver: An authorized relinquishment or modification of a requirement under applicable terms. / consent: Permission given by the relevant party for a specified action. / covenant: A contractual undertaking or restriction. / security document: A document establishing or recording relevant collateral rights. / collateral: Property or rights supporting repayment under an agreed arrangement. / guarantor: A party undertaking specified obligations relating to another party's debt. / facility agent: A party coordinating specified administrative functions under a facility agreement. / conditions checklist: A record tracking requirements and their evidence or status. / document index: A list linking required documents to their identifiers and locations. / version control: Management of document issues and their applicable status. / exception log: A record of unresolved departures or discrepancies. / funding request: A request for a loan disbursement under the applicable arrangement. / release authorization: Approval allowing a specified disbursement or action to proceed. / document retention: Preservation of records under the relevant requirements.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10, naming L73, C4, and the two different version references. Swap roles for turns 11-20; distinguish satisfaction, waiver, and release authorization. Complete the N26 transfer and verify each document and condition reference.
Partner: Ravi: Loan operations officer / Mae: Documentation reviewer
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Package N26 uses approved agreement version 5. The certificate supporting condition D2 refers to version 4. Satisfaction is unresolved, and the affected drawdown is not authorized under the stated process.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ravi: Would satisfying or appropriately waiving C4 automatically authorize release of the funds?
Mae: Check the separate release authorization requirements. Resolving this condition does not automatically establish every other required approval. Report the document decision and funding status separately so nobody infers permission from a narrower update.
Ravi: My handoff will show L73, C4, approved version 3, the certificate's version 2 reference, the unresolved status, and the authorized review request.
Mae: Good. Keep the funding request visible as well, but do not let its urgency replace the evidence. The next team needs the actual condition and authorization status, not a reassuring checkbox unsupported by the record.
END REFERENCE
Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Optional second-round challenge: Package N26 uses approved agreement version 5. The certificate supporting condition D2 refers to version 4. Satisfaction is unresolved, and the affected drawdown is not authorized under the stated process.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ravi: Would satisfying or appropriately waiving C4 automatically authorize release of the funds?
Mae: Check the separate release authorization requirements. Resolving this condition does not automatically establish every other required approval. Report the document decision and funding status separately so nobody infers permission from a narrower update.
Ravi: My handoff will show L73, C4, approved version 3, the certificate's version 2 reference, the unresolved status, and the authorized review request.
Mae: Good. Keep the funding request visible as well, but do not let its urgency replace the evidence. The next team needs the actual condition and authorization status, not a reassuring checkbox unsupported by the record.
END REFERENCE
04Payment Operations and ExceptionsReviewed is not the same as resolved+

Your goal

Give a payments-queue handoff with reconciled counts, clear stage labels, and no unsupported settlement claim.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Reviewed is not the same as resolved

At 10:00 local time, a fixed queue snapshot contains 45 payment exceptions. Thirty have been reviewed: 24 are resolved under the queue process, and six await the required authorization. Fifteen have not yet been reviewed. There are no other categories in this snapshot. Supervisor Bea asks analyst Colin whether all items are resolved. Colin must report 24 resolved and 21 still open, while distinguishing the review rate from the resolution rate. A resolved queue exception does not itself confirm that the underlying payment has settled. The team will provide another status snapshot at 11:00; resolution of every item is not promised.

Bea: Payments supervisor / Colin: Payments analyst

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many exceptions are resolved?

Answer and explanation

D. 24

Only twenty-four reviewed items are resolved; the other six reviewed items still await authorization.

2. How many exceptions remain open?

Answer and explanation

B. 21

Six reviewed but unresolved items plus fifteen unreviewed items give twenty-one open exceptions.

3. What does 11:00 represent?

Answer and explanation

D. The next queue snapshot

Eleven hundred is the next status report, not a guarantee of resolution, authorization, or settlement.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Thirty reviewed out of 45 is approximately 66.7%; 24 resolved out of 45 is approximately 53.3%. The difference is six reviewed items awaiting authorization. Open items total 21, not just the 15 unreviewed.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
payment instruction
A request specifying a transfer of funds.validate a payment instruction
payment rail
The system or network through which a payment is processed.identify the payment rail
Automated Clearing House (ACH)
A network supporting specified electronic credit and debit transfers.process an ACH payment
wire transfer
A funds transfer through the relevant wire-payment system.track a wire transfer
real-time gross settlement (RTGS)
Settlement of individual transfers in real time under the relevant system.distinguish RTGS processing
payment exception
An item requiring attention outside the normal processing path.investigate a payment exception
exception queue
A collection of payment items awaiting specified review or action.monitor the exception queue
queue snapshot
The queue's recorded status at a particular time.timestamp the queue snapshot
reviewed item
An item examined under the review process, possibly still unresolved.count reviewed items
resolved exception
An exception meeting the queue's defined resolution condition.record a resolved exception
pending authorization
A state awaiting required approval.identify pending authorization
unreviewed item
An item not yet examined under the stated process.prioritize unreviewed items
originator
The party initiating the payment under the relevant arrangement.identify the originator
beneficiary
The intended recipient of a payment.verify beneficiary details
routing information
Details directing the payment through the relevant institutions or system.validate routing information
value date
The date used for value or funds treatment under the relevant payment arrangement.confirm the value date
cut-off time
A deadline for a specified processing cycle or service.verify the cut-off time
payment release
The authorized sending of a payment to its next processing stage.confirm payment release
settlement
The discharge of the payment obligation through the relevant system.verify settlement status
return
A payment sent back under the applicable process.investigate a payment return
repair
Correction of a payment instruction through authorized procedures.route a payment repair
duplicate payment
A payment instruction or execution repeating another unintentionally or without the intended basis.review a possible duplicate payment
resolution rate
The proportion of the defined population meeting the resolution condition.calculate the resolution rate
review rate
The proportion of the defined population examined under the review process.calculate the review rate

Resolved describes the queue exception under its stated process. It does not automatically mean released, received, or settled. Different payment rails and states require their actual evidence; this snapshot supplies no settlement confirmation.

C Notice the language

Give the denominator with the good news

A busy manager may hear thirty reviewed as thirty finished. State the full population and the mutually exclusive status groups. Recognize completed review work without letting a positive headline hide items that still need authorization or examination.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Timestamp the report
This is the queue snapshot at 10:00 local time.
Give the population
There are 45 exceptions in this snapshot.
State completed review
Thirty items have been reviewed.
Separate the resolved group
Twenty-four are resolved under the queue process.
Name the remaining reviewed items
Six reviewed items await authorization.
State the unreviewed group
Fifteen have not yet been reviewed.
Reconcile the open count
Twenty-one exceptions remain open.
Explain the arithmetic
Six awaiting authorization plus fifteen unreviewed equals twenty-one.
Report the review rate
The review rate is approximately 66.7%.
Report the resolution rate
The resolution rate is approximately 53.3%.
Avoid a settlement claim
Queue resolution does not confirm payment settlement.
Keep authority explicit
The six items still require the relevant authorization.
Avoid silent relabeling
Do not count reviewed items as resolved.
Give the next contact
The next snapshot will be provided at 11:00.
Limit the commitment
That is a reporting time, not a promise to resolve every item.
Close the handoff
Keep the counts and stage labels attached to the same timestamp.
6 language and precision notes
Of the thirty
Makes clear that resolved and awaiting-authorization groups are subsets of reviewed items.
Still open
Includes more than the unreviewed portion of the queue.
At 10:00
Restricts a count to its snapshot rather than a changing live total.
Approximately
Signals rounding of the stated ratios.
Resolved under the process
Does not silently claim downstream settlement.
Next snapshot
Promises refreshed information, not completed processing.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which breakdown reconciles to 45?

Answer and explanation

A. 24 resolved, six awaiting authorization, fifteen unreviewed

The mutually exclusive groups sum to forty-five without double-counting the reviewed subset.

2. Which rate is approximately 66.7%?

Answer and explanation

A. Review rate

Thirty reviewed divided by forty-five total exceptions gives approximately sixty-six point seven percent.

3. Which statement improperly strengthens the evidence?

Answer and explanation

D. All 24 underlying payments have settled because their exceptions are resolved.

Exception resolution is not evidence of underlying payment settlement, which the snapshot does not confirm.

4. What must the next report preserve?

Answer and explanation

B. A common timestamp, denominator, and distinct stage labels

Comparable counts need the same snapshot basis and clear stages rather than mixed timestamps or unsupported outcome claims.

Check explanations in the book, page 91 ↗

E Conversations

Reviewed is not the same as resolved

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Bea: Payments supervisor / Colin: Payments analyst

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • resolution rate
  • cut-off time
  • exception queue
  • review rate
  • resolved exception
  • payment release
  • settlement
  • queue snapshot
  • Pending authorization
  • unreviewed item
  1. Bea
    The report says thirty items reviewed. Can I tell the morning meeting that the exception queue is cleared?
  2. Colin
    No. The 1 contains forty-five items at ten hundred local time. Thirty were reviewed, but only twenty-four are resolved. Six reviewed items still await authorization, and fifteen remain unreviewed.
  3. Bea
    I had read thirty reviewed as thirty finished. Show the six awaiting approval separately so the meeting does not repeat that mistake.
  4. Colin
    Yes. 2 is an open status here. Those six items cannot be called resolved simply because someone examined them. The review found another step that has not yet been completed.
  5. Bea
    How many exceptions are still open in total? I initially thought only the fifteen unreviewed items remained.
  6. Colin
    There are twenty-one. Each 3 belongs in the open group, along with the six awaiting authorization. Fifteen plus six gives twenty-one; counting only untouched items would understate the remaining work.
  7. Bea
    I want a clear percentage as well as counts. Thirty divided by forty-five is approximately sixty-six point seven percent.
  8. Colin
    That is the 4. The resolution rate uses twenty-four, not thirty, as the numerator. Both can be useful, but the label must match the status being counted.
  9. Bea
    Twenty-four divided by forty-five gives approximately fifty-three point three percent. We should not substitute the larger review percentage.
  10. Colin
    Correct. The 5 describes the queue's defined resolved state. It does not mean that the same percentage of all payment value has moved, because this snapshot counts items rather than money.
  11. Bea
    Do the twenty-four resolved exceptions establish that those payments have reached their final destination? The meeting may ask about customer impact.
  12. Colin
    They do not establish 6. Exception resolution and payment settlement are different states. We need the relevant payment records before making a statement about the underlying transfer's completion or customer receipt.
  13. Bea
    The approver expects to act shortly. I will leave those six open until the actual decision, even if it arrives before the meeting.
  14. Colin
    Right. A 7 must meet the stated condition, not an expectation. Keep the approval dependency explicit until its actual status changes under the authorized process.
  15. Bea
    If we report a processing deadline, we should specify which system and stage it concerns. There is no universal time for every payment.
  16. Colin
    Exactly. A 8 belongs to a particular service or cycle. This snapshot gives no applicable cut-off or settlement confirmation, so we must not invent one to make the update sound complete.
  17. Bea
    Let us provide another snapshot at eleven hundred. That gives the team a clear next contact without promising that all items will disappear.
  18. Colin
    Agreed. We can report any verified 9 information separately if available. Eleven hundred is our next information update, not an authorization event or a guarantee that every open exception will be resolved.
  19. Bea
    My headline will say forty-five total, twenty-four resolved, six reviewed awaiting authorization, and fifteen unreviewed, all as of ten hundred.
  20. Colin
    That accurately describes the 10. The three exclusive groups reconcile to forty-five, and the open count is twenty-one. Keep those counts with the timestamp so readers do not combine this snapshot with a later number.

Answers and explanations
  1. queue snapshot Turn 2. The queue snapshot fixes the population and status at 10:00, preventing a review count from becoming an all-clear claim.
  2. Pending authorization Turn 4. Pending authorization identifies reviewed items that still require approval and therefore remain unresolved in this queue.
  3. unreviewed item Turn 6. An unreviewed item is one of the fifteen not examined; open items also include the six reviewed but unresolved cases.
  4. review rate Turn 8. Review rate measures examined items over the full population, so thirty of forty-five is approximately 66.7%.
  5. resolution rate Turn 10. Resolution rate uses the twenty-four exceptions meeting the defined resolution condition, producing approximately 53.3% of the forty-five-item queue.
  6. settlement Turn 12. Settlement is a downstream payment state requiring its actual evidence; resolving a queue exception does not confirm it.
  7. resolved exception Turn 14. A resolved exception has actually met the queue's condition; expected authorization does not satisfy that condition in advance.
  8. cut-off time Turn 16. A cut-off time applies to a specified processing cycle or service; it is not a universal promise about every payment's settlement.
  9. payment release Turn 18. Payment release is the authorized sending to a next stage; it must be verified separately and not inferred from a reporting appointment.
  10. exception queue Turn 20. The exception queue is the defined population requiring attention; its status must preserve all three groups and their common timestamp.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Thirty reviewed does not mean thirty resolved

Fictional payment-operations update: 45 exceptions exist, 30 reviewed and 15 pending; resolution outcomes for the reviewed items are not supplied.

  1. Payments manager: We had forty-five exceptions, with thirty reviewed. Are all forty-five resolved now?
  2. Exception queue analyst: No. Fifteen are still awaiting review, and reviewed doesn't establish resolution for the other thirty.
  3. Payments manager: Then which progress figure can I accurately use in the operations meeting this morning?
  4. Exception queue analyst: Thirty reviews are complete and fifteen remain pending; resolution status needs its own verified count.
  5. Payments manager: Does the reviewed group include wire transfer and ACH items, or only one payment type?
  6. Exception queue analyst: That breakdown isn't supplied here. We should request it rather than infer the queue composition.
  7. Payments manager: I'll avoid saying sanctions screening is cleared just because an item received an initial review.
  8. Exception queue analyst: Correct. Each required status follows its authorized process and supporting evidence.
  9. Payments manager: Please obtain the resolution breakdown and identify which pending items need the next review contact.
  10. Exception queue analyst: I'll return verified counts and keep review completion separate from payment release or final resolution.
A batch is transmitted, acceptance is pending

Separate fictional payment case: an ACH file is transmitted; receiving-system acknowledgment is missing, and no settlement outcome is established.

  1. Payment operations coordinator: The ACH file was transmitted. Can we tell the business team the payments have settled?
  2. Processing analyst: No. Transmission is confirmed, but acknowledgment and the later payment status aren't established here.
  3. Payment operations coordinator: Then I should report the completed sending step and the missing receiving-system response separately.
  4. Processing analyst: Exactly. A file leaving our system doesn't prove acceptance or settlement at the destination.
  5. Payment operations coordinator: Should we send the file again immediately because we haven't received an acknowledgment yet?
  6. Processing analyst: Don't infer the appropriate action here. Follow the authorized exception process to avoid unsupported duplicate handling.
  7. Payment operations coordinator: I'll ask the responsible processing team for a status check and keep the uncertainty visible.
  8. Processing analyst: Good. Identify the file reference through the approved channel without sharing customer payment details broadly.
  9. Payment operations coordinator: The update will say transmitted, acknowledgment pending, and no confirmed settlement outcome yet.
  10. Processing analyst: That is useful progress reporting without turning a completed technical step into a financial guarantee.
A returned item still needs case follow-up

Separate fictional wire-transfer exception case: a return message is received, but the related account posting and customer communication remain unverified.

  1. Wire operations analyst: The return message arrived. Can I close the exception as completely resolved in today's summary?
  2. Payments team lead: Not yet. The related account posting and customer-communication status haven't been verified.
  3. Wire operations analyst: So return received is completed, while those follow-up checks remain open in the case.
  4. Payments team lead: Correct. We should avoid a single resolved label that hides unfinished work.
  5. Wire operations analyst: Can I tell the relationship manager the customer's funds are available again immediately?
  6. Payments team lead: That isn't established by the message alone. Obtain the authorized posting status before making that claim.
  7. Wire operations analyst: I'll ask the account-processing contact for the verified status and check who owns communication.
  8. Payments team lead: Good. Keep the return reason tied to its actual record, without inventing an explanation.
  9. Wire operations analyst: I'll report return received, posting verification pending, and communication ownership awaiting confirmation.
  10. Payments team lead: That describes the current exception queue accurately and points to the next work without premature closure.

Reviewed is not the same as resolved
  1. Read turns 1-10 and reconcile the 24, six, and fifteen status groups.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; distinguish resolving an exception from settling a payment.

Three awaiting authorization plus eight unreviewed gives eleven open exceptions.

Answer and explanation

B. 60%

Twelve reviewed divided by twenty total exceptions equals sixty percent.

4. Supervisor: "The resolution rate is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. 45%

Nine resolved divided by twenty total exceptions equals forty-five percent.

Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): This is the queue snapshot at 10:00 local time. / There are 45 exceptions in this snapshot. / Thirty items have been reviewed. / Twenty-four are resolved under the queue process. / Six reviewed items await authorization. / Fifteen have not yet been reviewed. / Twenty-one exceptions remain open. / Six awaiting authorization plus fifteen unreviewed equals twenty-one. / The review rate is approximately 66.7%. / The resolution rate is approximately 53.3%. / Queue resolution does not confirm payment settlement. / The six items still require the relevant authorization. / Do not count reviewed items as resolved. / The next snapshot will be provided at 11:00. / That is a reporting time, not a promise to resolve every item. / Keep the counts and stage labels attached to the same timestamp.
Vocabulary: payment instruction: A request specifying a transfer of funds. / payment rail: The system or network through which a payment is processed. / Automated Clearing House (ACH): A network supporting specified electronic credit and debit transfers. / wire transfer: A funds transfer through the relevant wire-payment system. / real-time gross settlement (RTGS): Settlement of individual transfers in real time under the relevant system. / payment exception: An item requiring attention outside the normal processing path. / exception queue: A collection of payment items awaiting specified review or action. / queue snapshot: The queue's recorded status at a particular time. / reviewed item: An item examined under the review process, possibly still unresolved. / resolved exception: An exception meeting the queue's defined resolution condition. / pending authorization: A state awaiting required approval. / unreviewed item: An item not yet examined under the stated process. / originator: The party initiating the payment under the relevant arrangement. / beneficiary: The intended recipient of a payment. / routing information: Details directing the payment through the relevant institutions or system. / value date: The date used for value or funds treatment under the relevant payment arrangement. / cut-off time: A deadline for a specified processing cycle or service. / payment release: The authorized sending of a payment to its next processing stage. / settlement: The discharge of the payment obligation through the relevant system. / return: A payment sent back under the applicable process. / repair: Correction of a payment instruction through authorized procedures. / duplicate payment: A payment instruction or execution repeating another unintentionally or without the intended basis. / resolution rate: The proportion of the defined population meeting the resolution condition. / review rate: The proportion of the defined population examined under the review process.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 and reconcile the 24, six, and fifteen status groups. Swap roles for turns 11-20; distinguish resolving an exception from settling a payment. Complete the twenty-item transfer and check the open count and both percentages.
Partner: Bea: Payments supervisor / Colin: Payments analyst
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A fixed snapshot has 20 exceptions. Twelve are reviewed: nine resolved and three awaiting authorization. Eight are unreviewed. There are no other categories.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Bea: Let us provide another snapshot at eleven hundred. That gives the team a clear next contact without promising that all items will disappear.
Colin: Agreed. We can report any verified payment release information separately if available. Eleven hundred is our next information update, not an authorization event or a guarantee that every open exception will be resolved.
Bea: My headline will say forty-five total, twenty-four resolved, six reviewed awaiting authorization, and fifteen unreviewed, all as of ten hundred.
Colin: That accurately describes the exception queue. The three exclusive groups reconcile to forty-five, and the open count is twenty-one. Keep those counts with the timestamp so readers do not combine this snapshot with a later number.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

05Fraud Operations and Customer EscalationGive one accurate explanation of the disputed-transaction process+

Your goal

Correct conflicting service messages while separating dispute intake, investigation, temporary credit, and the final outcome.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Give one accurate explanation of the disputed-transaction process

Customer June disputes a $380 debit-card transaction posted on 7 September to her U.S. consumer account. The bank received her notice on 9 September. One agent told her to resolve it with the merchant first; another said a police report was required before an investigation could begin. Specialist Amir must correct those barriers under the applicable electronic-fund-transfer error-resolution process, preserve the original notice date, and identify the information still needed. No final finding or credit decision is supplied. Applicable investigation, provisional-credit, and notice requirements must be checked promptly; a later handoff does not restart the notice date or justify delay.

June: Customer / Amir: Disputes specialist

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the original notice date?

Answer and explanation

A. 9 September

Nine September is when the bank received the notice; posting and internal handoff dates are different.

2. Which barriers must Amir correct in this case?

Answer and explanation

B. Requiring merchant resolution or a police report before beginning the applicable investigation

The supplied U.S. consumer dispute must not be blocked by those stated prerequisites to initiating error resolution.

3. What outcome has been established?

Answer and explanation

D. No final finding or credit decision is supplied.

The brief leaves both the final finding and credit decision unspecified.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

For this U.S. consumer electronic-transfer dispute, do not require merchant resolution or a police report before initiating the applicable investigation. Preserve the original 9 September notice; an internal handoff does not create a new notice date.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
electronic fund transfer (EFT)
A transfer of funds through electronic means within the applicable framework.identify the electronic fund transfer
Regulation E
A U.S. regulation implementing consumer electronic-fund-transfer protections.apply the relevant Regulation E process
unauthorized transaction
A transaction the customer did not make or permit, subject to the applicable definition.report an unauthorized transaction
disputed transaction
A transaction challenged by the customer for a stated reason.record the disputed transaction
notice of error
A customer's communication identifying an alleged error under the applicable process.preserve the notice of error
notice date
The date the relevant recipient received the error report.retain the original notice date
posting date
The date a transaction is recorded to the account.distinguish the posting date
merchant
The seller or service provider associated with a card transaction.identify the merchant
investigation
Examination of relevant facts to determine the dispute outcome.begin the applicable investigation
provisional credit
A temporary account credit while the relevant investigation continues.explain provisional credit
final credit
A credit made permanent under the completed decision.distinguish final credit
debit-card dispute
A challenge relating to a debit-card transaction.handle a debit-card dispute
error resolution
The process for investigating and addressing alleged account errors.follow error-resolution requirements
written confirmation
A written account confirming a previously reported matter when applicable.explain written-confirmation requirements
supporting information
Relevant details or records assisting the investigation.request supporting information
transaction descriptor
The displayed text identifying a transaction or merchant entry.check the transaction descriptor
case reference
The identifier assigned to the dispute or review.provide the case reference
handoff
Transfer of responsibility or information between service teams.document the handoff
interim update
A progress communication before the final decision.provide an interim update
final finding
The determination reached after the relevant investigation.explain the final finding
notification requirement
An obligation to communicate specified information under the applicable process.verify notification requirements
customer authentication
Verification of the customer's identity through the approved process.complete customer authentication
sensitive credential
Secret information used to access or authorize account activity.protect sensitive credentials
process correction
An action fixing an inaccurate instruction or handling step.record the process correction

Provisional credit is temporary and is distinct from a final finding or permanent credit. Applicable requirements and timing must be checked promptly. This exercise does not create a universal deadline or make provisional credit discretionary when the law requires it.

C Notice the language

Repair the process explanation before adding another request

A customer who has heard contradictory instructions may distrust even an accurate next step. Acknowledge the conflict, preserve the original report, and explain which information genuinely helps the review. Do not make the customer repeat an unnecessary prerequisite to receive a consistent answer.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the conflict
You received two different process explanations.
Correct the barriers
We should not make those prerequisites to initiating the applicable investigation.
Preserve the notice
The bank received your report on 9 September.
Separate the posting date
The transaction posted on 7 September.
Avoid restarting the clock
This handoff does not change the original notice date.
Identify the amount
The disputed debit-card amount is $380.
Request relevant facts
Please confirm the transaction descriptor and what you dispute.
Avoid repeated intake
I will check what the file already contains.
Keep authentication safe
Use the approved verification process; do not share a PIN or one-time code here.
Explain the review
The investigation will assess the relevant facts.
Separate temporary and final credit
Provisional credit does not establish the final outcome.
Avoid a premature guarantee
No permanent refund decision is supplied in the current record.
Respect applicable requirements
We need to check the investigation and credit requirements promptly.
Explain a real next step
I will identify the missing information and responsible review team.
Correct the record
Document the earlier conflicting instructions and the process correction.
Close clearly
Keep the original notice, investigation status, and credit status separate.
6 language and precision notes
Received on
Names the actual notice date rather than a later processing date.
Before beginning
Identifies the improper barriers supplied by this case.
Provisional
Temporary pending the relevant investigation and decision.
Not supplied
Prevents an invented credit decision from entering the exercise.
Promptly check
Does not authorize waiting indefinitely for internal convenience.
Do not share
Protects secret credentials while allowing approved identity verification.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which message corrects the process?

Answer and explanation

A. We will preserve your 9 September notice and not require those prerequisites to begin the applicable investigation.

The correct message preserves the original notice and removes the improper initiation barriers in this case.

2. What does provisional credit mean?

Answer and explanation

B. Temporary credit distinct from a final decision

Provisional credit is temporary during the applicable investigation and does not itself decide the final outcome.

3. Which date is the posting date?

Answer and explanation

B. 7 September

Seven September is explicitly when the disputed transaction posted to the account.

4. Which request should Amir avoid?

Answer and explanation

B. Asking June to disclose a PIN or one-time code in this conversation

Secret access credentials must be protected; approved authentication should not become a request to disclose them here.

Answers and explanations
  1. process correction Turn 2. Process correction removes the inaccurate initiation barriers without deciding the transaction's merits or promising a refund.
  2. notice date Turn 4. The notice date is September ninth, when the bank received the report; a later handoff does not restart it.
  3. transaction descriptor Turn 6. The transaction descriptor is the displayed identifying text and helps locate the disputed entry without establishing whether it was authorized.
  4. unauthorized transaction Turn 8. Unauthorized transaction describes the customer's reported concern subject to the applicable definition and factual investigation.
  5. Provisional credit Turn 10. Provisional credit is temporary during investigation and must not be described as a final permanent refund decision.
  6. error resolution Turn 12. Error resolution has requirements for investigation and communication; an open case is not permission to delay indefinitely.
  7. written confirmation Turn 14. Written confirmation requirements must be explained accurately within the applicable process, not invented as a universal barrier to investigation.
  8. Customer authentication Turn 16. Customer authentication uses the approved identity-checking process; it does not justify requesting secret credentials in this conversation.
  9. handoff Turn 18. The handoff transfers accurate context and responsibility while preserving the original notice, reported facts, and corrected process instructions.
  10. interim update Turn 20. An interim update reports progress without substituting for the final finding or altering applicable investigation and credit obligations.
Read the complete conversation

  7. June: I did not make or permit this transaction. Will you record that without assuming the result before the investigation?
  8. Amir: Yes. We will record the alleged unauthorized transaction and investigate the relevant facts. We must consider your report properly. The file contains no final finding.
  9. June: Another message mentioned temporary credit. Does that mean the bank has already decided to give me the money permanently?

  11. June: I understand that the outcome is open, but I need the team to follow the correct timetable rather than leave the case pending indefinitely.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Correcting conflicting dispute explanations

Fictional fraud-operations coordination: a customer received conflicting process descriptions; the dispute outcome remains unknown. The dialogue is between a complaints liaison and case specialist.

  1. Customer complaints liaison: The customer received conflicting explanations of the dispute process and now expects a guaranteed refund.
  2. Fraud case specialist: I'm sorry our communication created that expectation. The case outcome has not been determined.
  3. Customer complaints liaison: One message mentioned provisional credit, while another called the case fully resolved.
  4. Fraud case specialist: Those aren't interchangeable statuses. We need the verified case record before explaining what applies here.
  5. Customer complaints liaison: Can I promise that opening a fraud claim automatically produces provisional credit for this account?
  6. Fraud case specialist: No. Don't invent an entitlement or timing; use the applicable process and authorized case information.
  7. Customer complaints liaison: I'll acknowledge the conflicting wording and explain that the review remains open.
  8. Fraud case specialist: Good. Also avoid describing a chargeback as a guaranteed final recovery for the customer.
  9. Customer complaints liaison: I'll ask the case owner for the confirmed next step and provide one consistent correction.
  10. Fraud case specialist: I'll support that status check and keep the communication correction separate from the unresolved dispute decision.
A temporary credit was described as final

Separate fictional dispute case: the verified record labels a credit temporary, but a service message calls it final; no final case decision exists.

  1. Service quality officer: The record labels this credit temporary, but our message told the customer it was final.
  2. Dispute operations lead: We need to correct that directly. The message overstated the status in the verified record.
  3. Service quality officer: How can I explain the correction without suggesting the customer misunderstood accurate information?
  4. Dispute operations lead: Say we used the wrong description and that the underlying dispute review remains open.
  5. Service quality officer: Should I predict whether the temporary credit will remain after the review to reassure them?
  6. Dispute operations lead: No. The final outcome isn't established, and we must follow the actual account's authorized communication process.
  7. Service quality officer: I'll explain the recorded status and ask the case team for the applicable next-step wording.
  8. Dispute operations lead: Good. Keep any provisional-credit terms tied to this case rather than announce a universal banking rule.
  9. Service quality officer: I'll record the correction and make sure future messages don't describe the fraud claim as settled.
  10. Dispute operations lead: That repairs the inaccurate statement without replacing it with another unsupported promise about the customer's money.
A callback was mistaken for case completion

Separate fictional fraud-claim case: an agent promised completion during a scheduled callback, but the case team agreed only to provide a progress update.

  1. Relationship service officer: The customer expects the dispute to finish during tomorrow's callback because of my earlier message.
  2. Fraud operations coordinator: The case team agreed a progress update, not a completion date. That expectation needs correcting.
  3. Relationship service officer: I'll apologize for describing the call as a decision meeting when that wasn't confirmed.
  4. Fraud operations coordinator: Please explain what the call can provide: verified progress and any confirmed next step.
  5. Relationship service officer: Could I say the chargeback should succeed soon, even though the review is still open?
  6. Fraud operations coordinator: No. That would add an unsupported prediction and may create another expectation about recovery.
  7. Relationship service officer: I'll keep timing and outcome unconfirmed rather than use reassuring language that changes the facts.
  8. Fraud operations coordinator: Good. Ask the case owner to verify the status before the callback is made.
  9. Relationship service officer: I'll update our contact note so another agent doesn't repeat my original promise.
  10. Fraud operations coordinator: That gives the customer a consistent correction while leaving the fraud claim decision with the authorized process.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.
