Lesson 05: Fraud Operations and Customer Escalation
Original fictional case: Customer June disputes a $380 debit-card transaction posted on 7 September to her U.S. consumer account. The bank received her notice on 9 September. One agent told her to resolve it with the merchant first; another said a police report was required before an investigation could begin. Specialist Amir must correct those barriers under the applicable electronic-fund-transfer error-resolution process, preserve the original notice date, and identify the information still needed. No final finding or credit decision is supplied. Applicable investigation, provisional-credit, and notice requirements must be checked promptly; a later handoff does not restart the notice date or justify delay.
Language workshop: Give one accurate explanation of the disputed-transaction process
Communication goal: Correct conflicting service messages while separating dispute intake, investigation, temporary credit, and the final outcome.
Language guidance: For this U.S. consumer electronic-transfer dispute, do not require merchant resolution or a police report before initiating the applicable investigation. Preserve the original 9 September notice; an internal handoff does not create a new notice date.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You received two different process explanations. / We should not make those prerequisites to initiating the applicable investigation. / The bank received your report on 9 September. / The transaction posted on 7 September. / This handoff does not change the original notice date. / The disputed debit-card amount is $380. / Please confirm the transaction descriptor and what you dispute. / I will check what the file already contains. / Use the approved verification process; do not share a PIN or one-time code here. / The investigation will assess the relevant facts. / Provisional credit does not establish the final outcome. / No permanent refund decision is supplied in the current record. / We need to check the investigation and credit requirements promptly. / I will identify the missing information and responsible review team. / Document the earlier conflicting instructions and the process correction. / Keep the original notice, investigation status, and credit status separate.
Vocabulary: electronic fund transfer (EFT): A transfer of funds through electronic means within the applicable framework. / Regulation E: A U.S. regulation implementing consumer electronic-fund-transfer protections. / unauthorized transaction: A transaction the customer did not make or permit, subject to the applicable definition. / disputed transaction: A transaction challenged by the customer for a stated reason. / notice of error: A customer's communication identifying an alleged error under the applicable process. / notice date: The date the relevant recipient received the error report. / posting date: The date a transaction is recorded to the account. / merchant: The seller or service provider associated with a card transaction. / investigation: Examination of relevant facts to determine the dispute outcome. / provisional credit: A temporary account credit while the relevant investigation continues. / final credit: A credit made permanent under the completed decision. / debit-card dispute: A challenge relating to a debit-card transaction. / error resolution: The process for investigating and addressing alleged account errors. / written confirmation: A written account confirming a previously reported matter when applicable. / supporting information: Relevant details or records assisting the investigation. / transaction descriptor: The displayed text identifying a transaction or merchant entry. / case reference: The identifier assigned to the dispute or review. / handoff: Transfer of responsibility or information between service teams. / interim update: A progress communication before the final decision. / final finding: The determination reached after the relevant investigation. / notification requirement: An obligation to communicate specified information under the applicable process. / customer authentication: Verification of the customer's identity through the approved process. / sensitive credential: Secret information used to access or authorize account activity. / process correction: An action fixing an inaccurate instruction or handling step.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

06Operational Risk and ControlsA sign-off cannot erase an unmatched balance+

Your goal

Explain a reconciliation break, correct a misleading control status, and preserve evidence for the next reviewer.

This lesson in the book, page 49 ↗

A Read the situation

A sign-off cannot erase an unmatched balance

The 30 June reconciliation shows a $250,000 general-ledger balance and a $248,750 subledger balance on the same stated basis, leaving a $1,250 difference. The cause is unconfirmed. A control record has been signed as fully reconciled, although the unmatched item remains under investigation. Analyst Tess and controller Omar must correct that description through the traceable review process. The local procedure permits a reviewer to acknowledge a documented open exception, but not to label an unresolved difference fully matched. No journal adjustment or write-off has been approved. A timing difference is only one possible explanation, not an established fact.

Tess: Reconciliation analyst / Omar: Operations controller

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the unmatched amount?

Answer and explanation

C. $1,250

Two hundred fifty thousand minus two hundred forty-eight thousand seven hundred fifty equals twelve hundred fifty.

2. What is established about the cause?

Answer and explanation

B. It remains unconfirmed.

The brief explicitly leaves the cause unconfirmed and identifies timing only as a possibility.

3. What does the local procedure allow?

Answer and explanation

B. Acknowledgment of a documented open exception without calling it fully matched

The local process distinguishes review acknowledgment from claiming that an unresolved difference has been matched.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The $1,250 difference is established; its cause is not. Calling it a timing difference requires evidence. Neither the amount nor the existence of a signature authorizes an adjustment, write-off, or fully matched conclusion.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
general ledger (GL)
The main accounting record of balances and entries.reconcile the general ledger
subledger
A detailed record supporting a general-ledger balance.compare the subledger
reconciliation
Comparison of records to identify and explain differences.perform a reconciliation
reconciling item
A difference requiring explanation or resolution between compared records.investigate a reconciling item
unmatched balance
An amount not yet matched or explained on the comparison basis.report an unmatched balance
timing difference
A difference arising from when related events are recorded, if established.substantiate a timing difference
journal entry
An accounting entry recording a transaction or adjustment.review a journal entry
adjustment
A change to accounting records under the appropriate basis and authority.authorize an adjustment
write-off
Removal of a recorded amount under the applicable accounting decision.approve a write-off
supporting schedule
A detailed record explaining a reported balance.retain the supporting schedule
control sign-off
A recorded review or approval with a specified meaning.qualify the control sign-off
preparer
The person producing a record or reconciliation.identify the preparer
reviewer
The person checking the work under the relevant process.identify the reviewer
segregation of duties
Separation of incompatible responsibilities to reduce risk.maintain segregation of duties
maker-checker control
A process in which one person prepares and another independently checks the relevant action.apply a maker-checker control
control exception
A departure or unresolved condition identified in a control process.document a control exception
exception aging
The length of time an unresolved item has remained open.monitor exception aging
root-cause analysis
Examination of the underlying reason for a problem.conduct root-cause analysis
control deficiency
A weakness in control design or operation under the relevant assessment.assess a control deficiency
operational loss
Loss arising from relevant operational events or failures.record an operational loss
audit trail
Records allowing an action or decision to be traced.preserve the audit trail
reperformance
Independently repeating a procedure to evaluate its result.arrange reperformance
evidence reference
A link or identifier connecting a conclusion to supporting material.add an evidence reference
resolution owner
The person accountable for coordinating an item's resolution.assign a resolution owner

A reviewer may acknowledge an open exception under this stated local process, but the record must describe that status honestly. Correcting the label is different from resolving the underlying difference, and both need their own evidence.

C Notice the language

Describe the control state instead of defending the signature

A signed record can make a colleague reluctant to raise a remaining difference. Separate acknowledgment of work performed from confirmation that all items match. A precise correction protects the record without assuming misconduct by the person who signed it.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State the comparison
The ledger shows $250,000 and the subledger $248,750.
Confirm the basis
Both balances use the same stated comparison basis.
Calculate the difference
The unmatched amount is $1,250.
Preserve the unknown
The cause has not been confirmed.
Avoid a convenient label
Timing is a possible explanation, not an established one.
Correct the control description
Fully reconciled is not supported while this difference remains unexplained.
Recognize permitted acknowledgment
The reviewer can acknowledge a documented open exception.
Separate correction and resolution
Fixing the label does not resolve the balance difference.
Protect the evidence
Preserve the original record and correction history.
Avoid an unsupported entry
No balancing adjustment has been approved.
Keep authority explicit
A write-off requires its applicable basis and authorization.
Name responsibility
Assign the resolution owner and next review step.
Keep the item visible
Record the amount and status in the exception log.
Link the support
Attach the relevant ledger and subledger references.
Distinguish roles
Preparation and independent review are separate responsibilities.
Close accurately
Report the open difference and qualified review status together.
6 language and precision notes
Fully reconciled
Claims a completed match or explanation that this case lacks.
Acknowledged with exception
Can describe the local permitted review without hiding the difference.
Possible versus confirmed
Separates an explanation to investigate from an established cause.
Balancing entry
Must not be used merely to make unexplained records agree.
Corrected label
Changes the record's description, not the underlying balance.
Same basis
Ensures the subtraction compares like figures.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which control description is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. Reviewed with an open $1,250 exception; cause under investigation

The accurate status preserves the reviewed work and the outstanding difference without inventing its cause or resolution.

2. Which action is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

B. Post an unapproved $1,250 adjustment solely to force agreement.

No adjustment is authorized, and forcing agreement without a basis would conceal rather than explain the difference.

3. What is needed before calling this a timing difference?

Answer and explanation

A. Evidence establishing the timing explanation

Timing is a hypothesis until relevant records establish this difference's cause; precedent, sign-off, and size do not do so.

4. Which statement distinguishes two tasks correctly?

Answer and explanation

D. Correcting the sign-off description does not itself resolve the underlying balance.

Record correction and substantive reconciliation resolution are separate actions requiring their own basis.

Check explanations in the book, page 95 ↗

E Conversations

A sign-off cannot erase an unmatched balance

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Tess: Reconciliation analyst / Omar: Operations controller

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • journal entry
  • control sign-off
  • control exception
  • timing difference
  • resolution owner
  • audit trail
  • write-off
  • segregation of duties
  • unmatched balance
  • evidence reference
  1. Tess
    The June thirtieth record says fully reconciled, but the ledger shows two hundred fifty thousand and the subledger two hundred forty-eight thousand seven hundred fifty.
  2. Omar
    That leaves an 1 of twelve hundred fifty dollars. We must preserve that difference in the record. The signature does not establish that it has been explained or resolved.
  3. Tess
    The analyst thought it might be a posting-date issue. We do not yet have the records needed to confirm that explanation.
  4. Omar
    Then do not call it a confirmed 2. It is a possibility to investigate. A plausible explanation can guide the next check without becoming the stated cause before the evidence supports it.
  5. Tess
    Our procedure lets a reviewer acknowledge work with a documented exception. It does not permit calling every balance matched when one remains unresolved.
  6. Omar
    Correct the 3 accordingly. Reviewed with an open exception is different from fully reconciled. We should describe the permitted review state without using the signature to hide the unfinished item.
  7. Tess
    I will preserve the original signed record and document the correction. The status change should not silently remove the earlier wording.
  8. Omar
    Maintain the 4. Explain why the description changed and retain the supporting references. Correcting a misleading label improves the record, but it does not itself resolve the accounting difference.
  9. Tess
    The proposed adjustment would make the report balance, but I cannot find a transaction or approval supporting the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar entry.
  10. Omar
    Do not create a 5 simply to force agreement. Any actual adjustment needs its proper evidence and authority. Otherwise, the apparent match would conceal an unexplained item rather than provide a valid reconciliation.
  11. Tess
    Nor can we write it off just because it is smaller than the full account balance. The amount alone does not provide approval.
  12. Omar
    Exactly. A 6 requires its applicable basis and authorization. Neither has been established. Keep the difference open while the relevant records are examined, rather than selecting a convenient accounting outcome.
  13. Tess
    The note just says investigate. We need a named coordinator and the specific records to obtain before the next review.
  14. Omar
    Assign the 7 through the local process and record the next review step. Ownership, evidence gathering, and resolution are separate facts; an assigned name should not be mistaken for a closed issue.
  15. Tess
    We can link the ledger extract and detailed balance schedule. The next reviewer should see the actual amounts without reconstructing them from our conversation.
  16. Omar
    Add an 8 for each. Preserve the common comparison basis as well. If the balances come from different dates or scopes, that would be a different question requiring its own explanation.
  17. Tess
    The comparison basis matches. The root cause remains unconfirmed.
  18. Omar
    Keep the 9 visible with its amount and status. The control can acknowledge the exception honestly under the stated procedure; it cannot claim that no exception exists.
  19. Tess
    The corrected handoff will show the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar difference, unconfirmed cause, no approved adjustment, and review acknowledgment with the exception still open.
  20. Omar
    Good. Preserve 10 as the work proceeds. Independent review and any authorized accounting decision must follow the applicable roles. A cleaner-looking balance is not a substitute for an explained and properly approved outcome.

Answers and explanations
  1. unmatched balance Turn 2. The unmatched balance is the established 1,250-dollar difference on the stated common basis, not an explanation of its cause.
  2. timing difference Turn 4. A timing difference needs supporting evidence; it remains only one possible explanation in this case.
  3. control sign-off Turn 6. The control sign-off must state its actual meaning, distinguishing acknowledgment with an open exception from a fully matched conclusion.
  4. audit trail Turn 8. The audit trail preserves the original and subsequent correction so reviewers can trace what changed and why.
  5. journal entry Turn 10. A journal entry records an accounting event or adjustment; no unsupported balancing entry is authorized merely to force agreement.
  6. write-off Turn 12. A write-off requires the applicable accounting basis and authorization; relative size alone does not establish either.
  7. resolution owner Turn 14. The resolution owner coordinates follow-up on the difference; naming that person does not itself establish its cause or closure.
  8. evidence reference Turn 16. An evidence reference connects the stated balances and conclusions to the actual records, making the reconciliation reviewable.
  9. control exception Turn 18. The control exception is the unresolved condition in the process; recording it honestly does not determine whether or how it will be resolved.
  10. segregation of duties Turn 20. Segregation of duties keeps preparation, checking, and approval appropriately distinct rather than letting urgency bypass the required roles.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Tess: The June thirtieth record says fully reconciled, but the ledger shows two hundred fifty thousand and the subledger two hundred forty-eight thousand seven hundred fifty.
  2. Omar: That leaves an unmatched balance of twelve hundred fifty dollars. We must preserve that difference in the record. The signature does not establish that it has been explained or resolved.
  3. Tess: The analyst thought it might be a posting-date issue. We do not yet have the records needed to confirm that explanation.
  4. Omar: Then do not call it a confirmed timing difference. It is a possibility to investigate. A plausible explanation can guide the next check without becoming the stated cause before the evidence supports it.
  5. Tess: Our procedure lets a reviewer acknowledge work with a documented exception. It does not permit calling every balance matched when one remains unresolved.
  6. Omar: Correct the control sign-off accordingly. Reviewed with an open exception is different from fully reconciled. We should describe the permitted review state without using the signature to hide the unfinished item.
  7. Tess: I will preserve the original signed record and document the correction. The status change should not silently remove the earlier wording.
  8. Omar: Maintain the audit trail. Explain why the description changed and retain the supporting references. Correcting a misleading label improves the record, but it does not itself resolve the accounting difference.
  9. Tess: The proposed adjustment would make the report balance, but I cannot find a transaction or approval supporting the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar entry.
  10. Omar: Do not create a journal entry simply to force agreement. Any actual adjustment needs its proper evidence and authority. Otherwise, the apparent match would conceal an unexplained item rather than provide a valid reconciliation.
  11. Tess: Nor can we write it off just because it is smaller than the full account balance. The amount alone does not provide approval.
  12. Omar: Exactly. A write-off requires its applicable basis and authorization. Neither has been established. Keep the difference open while the relevant records are examined, rather than selecting a convenient accounting outcome.
  13. Tess: The note just says investigate. We need a named coordinator and the specific records to obtain before the next review.
  14. Omar: Assign the resolution owner through the local process and record the next review step. Ownership, evidence gathering, and resolution are separate facts; an assigned name should not be mistaken for a closed issue.
  15. Tess: We can link the ledger extract and detailed balance schedule. The next reviewer should see the actual amounts without reconstructing them from our conversation.
  16. Omar: Add an evidence reference for each. Preserve the common comparison basis as well. If the balances come from different dates or scopes, that would be a different question requiring its own explanation.
  17. Tess: The comparison basis matches. The root cause remains unconfirmed.
  18. Omar: Keep the control exception visible with its amount and status. The control can acknowledge the exception honestly under the stated procedure; it cannot claim that no exception exists.
  19. Tess: The corrected handoff will show the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar difference, unconfirmed cause, no approved adjustment, and review acknowledgment with the exception still open.
  20. Omar: Good. Preserve segregation of duties as the work proceeds. Independent review and any authorized accounting decision must follow the applicable roles. A cleaner-looking balance is not a substitute for an explained and properly approved outcome.
Dialogue in the book, page 55 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Signed off does not mean every balance matched

Fictional operational-control discussion: a reconciliation is signed off while one unmatched balance remains under investigation; sign-off meaning has not been clarified.

  1. Operations reporting manager: The reconciliation is signed off, so can I say every balance has been matched?
  2. Control analyst: One unmatched balance remains under investigation. We need to clarify what the sign-off actually records.
  3. Operations reporting manager: Could you explain reconciliation in plain English for the colleague preparing the management slide?
  4. Control analyst: It's a comparison of records to identify differences; signing a review doesn't necessarily remove every difference.
  5. Operations reporting manager: Then completing a review and resolving an unmatched balance are distinct statuses in this case.
  6. Control analyst: Exactly. The current label shouldn't hide the open difference or its assigned follow-up.
  7. Operations reporting manager: Does that open item prove the control never operated or that someone committed fraud?
  8. Control analyst: Neither conclusion follows automatically. We need evidence about operation, findings, and the investigation.
  9. Operations reporting manager: I'll explain the open balance and request clarification of the sign-off and remediation status.
  10. Control analyst: Good. That communicates operational risk accurately without treating a signature as proof of a fully reconciled position.
A control description is not evidence of operation

Separate fictional risk review: a team has documented a daily control but has not supplied records showing it operated during the review period.

  1. Risk coordinator: The daily control is described in the manual. Does that prove it operated throughout the review period?
  2. Control assurance specialist: No. The description explains what should happen; operating evidence shows what actually happened.
  3. Risk coordinator: So a written control design and documented performance are different things we need to explain.
  4. Control assurance specialist: Yes. We should request relevant records rather than infer completed checks from the manual.
  5. Risk coordinator: Would missing evidence prove the team never performed any of those daily checks?
  6. Control assurance specialist: Not by itself. It establishes a gap in the evidence supplied, which needs further review.
  7. Risk coordinator: I'll describe the manual as available and the operating-evidence assessment as still open.
  8. Control assurance specialist: Good. Keep any finding or remediation plan tied to the actual review, not assumptions about staff.
  9. Risk coordinator: I'll ask the record owner for the specified period's support through the approved channel.
  10. Control assurance specialist: That distinction helps management understand both the documented process and what remains unverified about its operation.
Fixing one item versus changing the process

Separate fictional operational-risk case: one erroneous internal entry has been corrected, but the process that produced it has not yet been assessed.

  1. Operations supervisor: We corrected the entry. Can we say the operational-risk issue has been permanently remediated?
  2. Risk and controls analyst: The individual error is corrected, but the contributing process hasn't been assessed yet.
  3. Operations supervisor: How would you explain the difference between that correction and broader remediation in ordinary language?
  4. Risk and controls analyst: One fixes this item; broader work addresses the process and checks whether the response is effective.
  5. Operations supervisor: Then the corrected entry is real progress without proving that similar errors cannot recur.
  6. Risk and controls analyst: Exactly. We should preserve that distinction and avoid a guarantee about all future activity.
  7. Operations supervisor: Should we immediately invent a new control based only on this one observation?
  8. Risk and controls analyst: The authorized review should assess causes and appropriate responses. This conversation doesn't establish a control design.
  9. Operations supervisor: I'll report the item correction and request the process-review status from its owner.
  10. Risk and controls analyst: Good. That separates a completed case action from the still-open assessment of systemic risk and remediation.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

A sign-off cannot erase an unmatched balance
  1. Read turns 1-10 and state the $1,250 difference without assigning an unconfirmed cause.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; separate correction of the sign-off from correction of a balance.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Describe a second reconciliation break

A same-basis comparison shows a $90,000 ledger balance and an $89,600 subledger balance. The cause is unknown. No adjustment is approved.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Analyst: "The ledger balance is ___ dollars."

Answer and explanation

B. 90,000

Ninety thousand is the supplied general-ledger amount for this separate comparison.

2. Reviewer: "The subledger balance is ___ dollars."

Answer and explanation

C. 89,600

Eighty-nine thousand six hundred is the supplied supporting balance.

3. Analyst: "The unmatched difference is ___ dollars."

Answer and explanation

B. 400

Ninety thousand minus eighty-nine thousand six hundred equals four hundred dollars.

4. Reviewer: "The cause remains ___."

Answer and explanation

A. unknown

The case does not establish a timing, error, or other explanation.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Lesson 06: Operational Risk and Controls
Original fictional case: The 30 June reconciliation shows a $250,000 general-ledger balance and a $248,750 subledger balance on the same stated basis, leaving a $1,250 difference. The cause is unconfirmed. A control record has been signed as fully reconciled, although the unmatched item remains under investigation. Analyst Tess and controller Omar must correct that description through the traceable review process. The local procedure permits a reviewer to acknowledge a documented open exception, but not to label an unresolved difference fully matched. No journal adjustment or write-off has been approved. A timing difference is only one possible explanation, not an established fact.
Language workshop: A sign-off cannot erase an unmatched balance
Communication goal: Explain a reconciliation break, correct a misleading control status, and preserve evidence for the next reviewer.
Language guidance: The $1,250 difference is established; its cause is not. Calling it a timing difference requires evidence. Neither the amount nor the existence of a signature authorizes an adjustment, write-off, or fully matched conclusion.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The ledger shows $250,000 and the subledger $248,750. / Both balances use the same stated comparison basis. / The unmatched amount is $1,250. / The cause has not been confirmed. / Timing is a possible explanation, not an established one. / Fully reconciled is not supported while this difference remains unexplained. / The reviewer can acknowledge a documented open exception. / Fixing the label does not resolve the balance difference. / Preserve the original record and correction history. / No balancing adjustment has been approved. / A write-off requires its applicable basis and authorization. / Assign the resolution owner and next review step. / Record the amount and status in the exception log. / Attach the relevant ledger and subledger references. / Preparation and independent review are separate responsibilities. / Report the open difference and qualified review status together.
Vocabulary: general ledger (GL): The main accounting record of balances and entries. / subledger: A detailed record supporting a general-ledger balance. / reconciliation: Comparison of records to identify and explain differences. / reconciling item: A difference requiring explanation or resolution between compared records. / unmatched balance: An amount not yet matched or explained on the comparison basis. / timing difference: A difference arising from when related events are recorded, if established. / journal entry: An accounting entry recording a transaction or adjustment. / adjustment: A change to accounting records under the appropriate basis and authority. / write-off: Removal of a recorded amount under the applicable accounting decision. / supporting schedule: A detailed record explaining a reported balance. / control sign-off: A recorded review or approval with a specified meaning. / preparer: The person producing a record or reconciliation. / reviewer: The person checking the work under the relevant process. / segregation of duties: Separation of incompatible responsibilities to reduce risk. / maker-checker control: A process in which one person prepares and another independently checks the relevant action. / control exception: A departure or unresolved condition identified in a control process. / exception aging: The length of time an unresolved item has remained open. / root-cause analysis: Examination of the underlying reason for a problem. / control deficiency: A weakness in control design or operation under the relevant assessment. / operational loss: Loss arising from relevant operational events or failures. / audit trail: Records allowing an action or decision to be traced. / reperformance: Independently repeating a procedure to evaluate its result. / evidence reference: A link or identifier connecting a conclusion to supporting material. / resolution owner: The person accountable for coordinating an item's resolution.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 and state the $1,250 difference without assigning an unconfirmed cause. Swap roles for turns 11-20; separate correction of the sign-off from correction of a balance. Complete the $90,000 comparison and check the amount, direction, and unknown cause.
Partner: Tess: Reconciliation analyst / Omar: Operations controller
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A same-basis comparison shows a $90,000 ledger balance and an $89,600 subledger balance. The cause is unknown. No adjustment is approved.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Tess: The comparison basis matches. The root cause remains unconfirmed.
Omar: Keep the control exception visible with its amount and status. The control can acknowledge the exception honestly under the stated procedure; it cannot claim that no exception exists.
Tess: The corrected handoff will show the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar difference, unconfirmed cause, no approved adjustment, and review acknowledgment with the exception still open.
Omar: Good. Preserve segregation of duties as the work proceeds. Independent review and any authorized accounting decision must follow the applicable roles. A cleaner-looking balance is not a substitute for an explained and properly approved outcome.
END REFERENCE
Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Optional second-round challenge: A same-basis comparison shows a $90,000 ledger balance and an $89,600 subledger balance. The cause is unknown. No adjustment is approved.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Tess: The comparison basis matches. The root cause remains unconfirmed.
Omar: Keep the control exception visible with its amount and status. The control can acknowledge the exception honestly under the stated procedure; it cannot claim that no exception exists.
Tess: The corrected handoff will show the twelve-hundred-fifty-dollar difference, unconfirmed cause, no approved adjustment, and review acknowledgment with the exception still open.
Omar: Good. Preserve segregation of duties as the work proceeds. Independent review and any authorized accounting decision must follow the applicable roles. A cleaner-looking balance is not a substitute for an explained and properly approved outcome.

07Audit and Regulatory ExamsA policy describes the control but does not prove it operated+

Your goal

Respond to an evidence request with exact scope, honest availability, and traceable period coverage.

This lesson in the book, page 58 ↗

A Read the situation

A policy describes the control but does not prove it operated

An examiner requests evidence that control R9 operated during March, including records for 3, 10, 17, 24, and 31 March. Coordinator Liv has policy version 2 ready. She has also retrieved completed records for 10 and 24 March; records for the other three requested dates have not yet been located. The absence of retrieved records does not by itself establish whether the control failed or operated. Control owner Yusuf will help locate the evidence and report retrieval status Wednesday at 13:00. Liv must distinguish policy design from operating evidence and must not recreate historical sign-offs as if they were original.

Liv: Examination coordinator / Yusuf: Control owner

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many requested dated records are retrieved?

Answer and explanation

A. Two of five

The records for 10 and 24 March are retrieved; three other requested dates remain unlocated.

2. What does policy version 2 primarily describe?

Answer and explanation

C. The control's documented design or requirements

A policy describes intended control arrangements and is distinct from evidence that the control actually operated.

3. What can be concluded about the three unlocated records?

Answer and explanation

A. Their retrieval is incomplete; operation is not established by that fact alone.

Unlocated evidence leaves a gap but does not alone prove either performance or failure.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Two retrieved records out of five requested dates is 40% retrieval coverage for that request. It is not a 40% control-effectiveness score, and it does not establish a conclusion about every day in March.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
regulatory examination
A supervisory review by the relevant regulatory authority.coordinate a regulatory examination
examination request
A request for records or explanation within an examination.respond to an examination request
request reference
The identifier linking a request to the response and evidence.retain the request reference
control design
The planned structure and requirements of a control.explain control design
operating effectiveness
Whether a control actually functions as intended over the relevant basis.assess operating effectiveness
population
The full set of items relevant to a defined review.define the review population
sample selection
The items chosen from the relevant population for examination.confirm the sample selection
review period
The dates covered by the requested assessment.identify the review period
dated evidence
A record tied to an identifiable time or event.provide dated evidence
execution record
Documentation that a specified activity was performed.retrieve the execution record
reviewer sign-off
A record of the reviewer's check or approval under the process.verify the reviewer sign-off
evidence inventory
A list of requested, available, and outstanding supporting records.maintain an evidence inventory
evidence gap
Missing support for a requested fact or conclusion.disclose an evidence gap
retrieval status
The stage of locating and obtaining records.report retrieval status
record custodian
The person or function responsible for keeping the relevant records.contact the record custodian
walkthrough
A guided examination of how a process is designed or carried out.conduct a process walkthrough
test of control
A procedure assessing relevant control design or operation.perform a test of control
period coverage
The extent to which evidence represents the required time interval.assess period coverage
contemporaneous record
A record created at or near the time of the relevant event.preserve a contemporaneous record
retrospective explanation
A later account describing a past event or missing record.label a retrospective explanation
evidence mapping
Linking each request item to its supporting record.complete evidence mapping
response package
The organized material submitted in reply to a request.assemble the response package
examiner conclusion
The outcome reached by the examiner's assessment.distinguish the examiner conclusion
management representation
A statement by management about a matter under review.qualify a management representation

A later explanation can be useful if clearly labeled and supported, but it is not an original historical sign-off. Preserve dates, authorship, and the distinction between contemporaneous evidence and a retrospective account.

C Notice the language

A transparent evidence gap is better than a misleading package

Teams may feel pressure to answer an examination request with whatever looks complete. Map the request to the exact dates and evidence types. A clear account of what is available and missing helps the reviewer understand the actual record without a fabricated claim of completeness.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Restate the request
The examiner requested R9 evidence for five specified March dates.
Identify the policy
Policy version 2 is available.
Separate design and operation
The policy describes requirements; it does not prove each execution.
State retrieved records
We have the records for 10 and 24 March.
Name the missing dates
Records for 3, 17, and 31 March are not yet located.
Quantify only what is measured
Retrieval coverage is two of five requested records.
Avoid a performance score
That is not a control-effectiveness percentage.
Preserve uncertainty
Missing retrieved evidence does not by itself prove performance or failure.
Map the package
Link each requested date to its actual evidence.
Contact the custodian
Yusuf will help locate the outstanding records.
Protect historical accuracy
Do not create a later sign-off as though it were original.
Label later explanations
Identify any retrospective account by author, date, and basis.
State the next contact
Retrieval status will be updated Wednesday at 13:00.
Limit the commitment
That is an update time, not a guarantee all records will be found.
Keep the conclusion separate
The examiner's assessment is not ours to invent.
Close the response
Submit the available evidence with an accurate inventory of outstanding items.
6 language and precision notes
Available policy
Describes a document's presence, not actual control performance.
For five dates
Limits the requested sample rather than implying every March day.
Not yet located
Names retrieval status without asserting that the activity never happened.
Forty percent retrieved
Measures record availability for this request, not effectiveness.
Retrospective
A later explanation must not masquerade as a contemporaneous entry.
Examiner conclusion
Belongs to the actual assessment, not a self-awarded status label.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which package description is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. Policy version 2 plus records for 10 and 24 March; three requested dates outstanding

The description accurately identifies available material and outstanding requested records without fabricating completeness or performance.

2. What is the retrieval coverage?

Answer and explanation

A. 40% of the five requested dated records

Two retrieved records divided by five requested dates gives forty percent retrieval coverage only.

3. Which action would misrepresent the evidence?

Answer and explanation

B. Create today's sign-off and present it as an original March record.

A later record must not be presented as contemporaneous evidence that was created in March.

4. What should Yusuf report Wednesday if records remain missing?

Answer and explanation

A. The actual retrieval status and outstanding dates

The promise concerns an accurate status update even if the evidence-retrieval work remains incomplete.

Check explanations in the book, page 97 ↗

E Conversations

A policy describes the control but does not prove it operated

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Liv: Examination coordinator / Yusuf: Control owner

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank

  • contemporaneous record
  • Operating effectiveness
  • evidence inventory
  • examiner conclusion
  • retrospective explanation
  • retrieval status

  1. Liv
    The examiner wants R9 records for March third, tenth, seventeenth, twenty-fourth, and thirty-first. I have policy version 2 ready.
  2. Yusuf
    The policy describes 1, but the request also needs operating evidence. We must distinguish the intended procedure from records showing what actually happened on each requested date.
  3. Liv
    I found records for the tenth and twenty-fourth. The other three are not yet located.
  4. Yusuf
    Put that in the 2. We have two requested dated records, not five. Keep the available records and missing dates visible rather than describing the package as complete because a policy document is attached.
  5. Liv
    That gives two out of five, or forty percent retrieval coverage. I should not call it a forty-percent effectiveness result.
  6. Yusuf
    Correct. 3 is a different assessment. Record availability for this request does not establish the control's success rate, and the five selected dates do not automatically represent every day in March.
  7. Liv
    Do the missing records prove the control was not performed on those dates? I want to avoid minimizing the gap.
  8. Yusuf
    They establish an 4, not the full explanation. We must investigate and report it honestly. Do not assume performance, but do not claim failure solely because the record has not yet been located.
  9. Liv
    I will map each requested date to the available file or outstanding status. That should help the examiner see what the response actually contains.
  10. Yusuf
    Use 5 with the relevant references. Check that each file belongs to the requested control and date. A similarly named record from another period is not a substitute merely because it is available.
  11. Liv
    Who can help find the outstanding files? I have checked the shared folder but have not established whether another authorized repository holds them.
  12. Yusuf
    I will coordinate with the 6. We need the actual retention and storage context, not a guess. Any retrieval route must preserve the records and follow the applicable access requirements.
  13. Liv
    Someone offered backdated sign-offs. That would make today's entries look like original March evidence.
  14. Yusuf
    Do not present a new entry as a 7. A later sign-off cannot be passed off as an original March record. We need accurate creation dates, authorship, and the actual basis of any later account.
  15. Liv
    A reviewer might remember performing the check. Could a clearly dated explanation help, even though it is not the original execution record?
  16. Yusuf
    A 8 can be evaluated for what it is. Identify its author, date, and supporting basis. Do not imply it has the same origin as the missing historical document or automatically resolves the request.
  17. Liv
    At Wednesday's update, I will name any dates still outstanding. I cannot promise the search will recover every record.
  18. Yusuf
    Correct. The 9 update will name any remaining gaps. If we cannot locate a record, we must say so clearly and follow the authorized response process rather than manufacture completeness.
  19. Liv
    The package will include the policy, two dated records, a mapped list of the three outstanding dates, and any properly labeled later explanation.
  20. Yusuf
    Good. Keep the 10 separate from our submission status. Providing a transparent package supports the review, but it does not mean the examiner has accepted the evidence or concluded that the control operated effectively.

Answers and explanations
  1. control design Turn 2. Control design states how the control should work; the policy alone does not establish operation on the requested dates.
  2. evidence inventory Turn 4. The evidence inventory lists available and outstanding records, preserving the two retrieved dates and three remaining gaps.
  3. Operating effectiveness Turn 6. Operating effectiveness concerns actual control performance; the percentage of retrieved records is not a score of that performance.
  4. evidence gap Turn 8. An evidence gap means support is missing; it does not by itself prove either that the control operated or that it failed.
  5. evidence mapping Turn 10. Evidence mapping links each requested item to its supporting record or explicit outstanding status, preventing a misleading package-level claim.
  6. record custodian Turn 12. The record custodian is responsible for relevant records and can assist retrieval without inventing evidence or guaranteeing its existence.
  7. contemporaneous record Turn 14. A contemporaneous record was created at or near the event; a new sign-off cannot be represented as original March evidence.
  8. retrospective explanation Turn 16. A retrospective explanation is a later account that must be labeled as such, not substituted deceptively for historical evidence.
  9. retrieval status Turn 18. Retrieval status reports what has actually been located; the update appointment does not guarantee that every missing record will be found.
  10. examiner conclusion Turn 20. The examiner conclusion follows the actual supervisory assessment; the team must not invent approval from its own document submission.

Dialogue in the book, page 64 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Request evidence for the stated examination period

Fictional examination preparation: a request asks for control-operation evidence over a stated period; only the policy document is ready.

  1. Examination coordinator: Could you provide evidence that the control operated during the period specified in the examiner's request?
  2. Operations control owner: The policy is ready. Do you need something beyond the description of how the control should work?
  3. Examination coordinator: Yes. The request concerns actual operation, so we need the relevant records for that stated period.
  4. Operations control owner: Can I send today's completed check as proof that all earlier checks were performed?
  5. Examination coordinator: Not as proof of the whole period. Match the evidence to the request and identify any gaps.
  6. Operations control owner: I'll check available records and confirm realistic retrieval timing through the approved review channel.
  7. Examination coordinator: Thank you. Please keep original dates and avoid recreating missing checks as if contemporaneous.
  8. Operations control owner: Does an evidence gap automatically become a final finding in the management response?
  9. Examination coordinator: That outcome belongs to the review. Our immediate task is an accurate, specific evidence response.
  10. Operations control owner: I'll provide the available support, missing portions, and retrieval status without overstating completeness.
The sample is ready but its approvals are missing

Separate fictional internal audit: transaction samples are collected, but linked review approvals requested by the auditor are not included.

  1. Audit liaison: Could you provide the linked review approvals for the collected sample, as requested in the audit evidence list?
  2. Operations records officer: We already supplied the sample transactions. Which part of the request remains unanswered?
  3. Audit liaison: The evidence of review approval for those same items, not a new unrelated sample.
  4. Operations records officer: I'll ask the record owner for the linked approvals and identify any that cannot be retrieved.
  5. Audit liaison: Please maintain the sample references so the reviewer can match each record to the original request.
  6. Operations records officer: Should I add an approval now if the reviewer remembers checking the item at the time?
  7. Audit liaison: Don't create retrospective evidence as an original record. Follow the authorized process for explaining any gap.
  8. Operations records officer: I'll distinguish available documentation from recollections and ask the audit lead how to present the latter.
  9. Audit liaison: Good. Confirm an achievable response point after checking availability rather than promising a complete bundle immediately.
  10. Operations records officer: I'll send the verified linked records through the approved channel with outstanding evidence clearly identified.
Ask what the management response still needs

Separate fictional examination follow-up: a draft management response says action taken but omits the action owner and supporting evidence; no finding closure is confirmed.

  1. Regulatory response coordinator: Could you identify the action owner and supporting evidence behind the draft phrase action taken?
  2. Operations remediation lead: I can provide the owner, but the evidence collection hasn't finished for this response.
  3. Regulatory response coordinator: Please give the confirmed ownership now and explain precisely which supporting records remain pending.
  4. Operations remediation lead: Would calling the finding closed make the response more decisive while we gather those records?
  5. Regulatory response coordinator: No. We don't have a confirmed closure, and the wording shouldn't exceed the verified status.
  6. Operations remediation lead: I'll request the missing evidence from its source and ask when it can be provided.
  7. Regulatory response coordinator: Good. The management response needs a clear action, purpose, and realistic timing for outstanding support.
  8. Operations remediation lead: Should I promise the examiner will accept the action once the bundle is complete?
  9. Regulatory response coordinator: No. Completeness of submission doesn't establish the examiner's assessment or acceptance.
  10. Operations remediation lead: I'll provide the verified action details and outstanding evidence status without predicting the review outcome.

A policy describes the control but does not prove it operated

Map another evidence request

An examiner requests four April records. Three are retrieved; one remains unlocated. The current policy is available. A retrieval update is due Friday.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Coordinator: "The number of requested dated records is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. four

Four is the requested population for this separate evidence-retrieval task.

2. Owner: "The number retrieved is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. three

Three records are actually available; the fourth remains unlocated.

3. Coordinator: "Retrieval coverage is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. 75%

Three retrieved divided by four requested equals seventy-five percent, not a control-effectiveness score.

4. Owner: "The next retrieval update is due ___."

Answer and explanation

C. Friday

Friday is the status-update commitment, not a guarantee of complete evidence or examiner approval.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Language guidance: Two retrieved records out of five requested dates is 40% retrieval coverage for that request. It is not a 40% control-effectiveness score, and it does not establish a conclusion about every day in March.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The examiner requested R9 evidence for five specified March dates. / Policy version 2 is available. / The policy describes requirements; it does not prove each execution. / We have the records for 10 and 24 March. / Records for 3, 17, and 31 March are not yet located. / Retrieval coverage is two of five requested records. / That is not a control-effectiveness percentage. / Missing retrieved evidence does not by itself prove performance or failure. / Link each requested date to its actual evidence. / Yusuf will help locate the outstanding records. / Do not create a later sign-off as though it were original. / Identify any retrospective account by author, date, and basis. / Retrieval status will be updated Wednesday at 13:00. / That is an update time, not a guarantee all records will be found. / The examiner's assessment is not ours to invent. / Submit the available evidence with an accurate inventory of outstanding items.
Vocabulary: regulatory examination: A supervisory review by the relevant regulatory authority. / examination request: A request for records or explanation within an examination. / request reference: The identifier linking a request to the response and evidence. / control design: The planned structure and requirements of a control. / operating effectiveness: Whether a control actually functions as intended over the relevant basis. / population: The full set of items relevant to a defined review. / sample selection: The items chosen from the relevant population for examination. / review period: The dates covered by the requested assessment. / dated evidence: A record tied to an identifiable time or event. / execution record: Documentation that a specified activity was performed. / reviewer sign-off: A record of the reviewer's check or approval under the process. / evidence inventory: A list of requested, available, and outstanding supporting records. / evidence gap: Missing support for a requested fact or conclusion. / retrieval status: The stage of locating and obtaining records. / record custodian: The person or function responsible for keeping the relevant records. / walkthrough: A guided examination of how a process is designed or carried out. / test of control: A procedure assessing relevant control design or operation. / period coverage: The extent to which evidence represents the required time interval. / contemporaneous record: A record created at or near the time of the relevant event. / retrospective explanation: A later account describing a past event or missing record. / evidence mapping: Linking each request item to its supporting record. / response package: The organized material submitted in reply to a request. / examiner conclusion: The outcome reached by the examiner's assessment. / management representation: A statement by management about a matter under review.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 and map each of the five requested dates to its actual status. Swap roles for turns 11-20; distinguish an original record from a later explanation. Complete the April transfer and label its 75% as retrieval coverage, not effectiveness.
Partner: Liv: Examination coordinator / Yusuf: Control owner
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: An examiner requests four April records. Three are retrieved; one remains unlocated. The current policy is available. A retrieval update is due Friday.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Liv: At Wednesday's update, I will name any dates still outstanding. I cannot promise the search will recover every record.
Yusuf: Correct. The retrieval status update will name any remaining gaps. If we cannot locate a record, we must say so clearly and follow the authorized response process rather than manufacture completeness.
Liv: The package will include the policy, two dated records, a mapped list of the three outstanding dates, and any properly labeled later explanation.
Yusuf: Good. Keep the examiner conclusion separate from our submission status. Providing a transparent package supports the review, but it does not mean the examiner has accepted the evidence or concluded that the control operated effectively.
END REFERENCE

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

08Customer Complaints and Fair TreatmentThe current fee schedule may not explain a past charge+

Your goal

Investigate a historical fee question using the applicable period, product, transaction, and waiver conditions.

This lesson in the book, page 67 ↗

A Read the situation

The current fee schedule may not explain a past charge

Customer Priya asks about a $15 maintenance charge for the June statement cycle. Representative Daniel has a current fee schedule effective 1 August showing a $10 maintenance fee for a particular account product. He has not checked which product Priya held in June, the terms effective for that cycle, or whether any waiver condition applied. He cannot yet establish whether the $15 charge was correct or whether a refund is due. Daniel will obtain the relevant account and historical fee records and update Priya Tuesday at 15:00. The current schedule must not be applied retroactively merely because it is the first document available.

Priya: Customer / Daniel: Customer-service representative

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What charge is disputed?

Answer and explanation

C. A $15 maintenance charge for the June cycle

Priya asks about fifteen dollars charged for the June statement cycle.

2. What is the effective date of Daniel's available schedule?

Answer and explanation

A. 1 August

The schedule is explicitly effective from 1 August and is not automatically applicable to June.

3. What remains unchecked?

Answer and explanation

A. June product, applicable historical terms, and waiver eligibility

The missing evidence concerns the actual account and applicable terms, not arithmetic or the order of months.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The current $10 schedule does not prove the June charge should have been $10. Its effective date is 1 August, and its product coverage still needs checking. A $5 difference alone does not establish a refund.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
maintenance fee
A recurring account charge under the applicable terms.review a maintenance fee
fee schedule
A document listing charges and their conditions.check the fee schedule
effective date
The date from which specified terms apply.confirm the effective date
statement cycle
The period covered by a periodic account statement.identify the statement cycle
account product
The specific type or version of account held by a customer.verify the account product
historical terms
The terms applicable during a past relevant period.retrieve historical terms
waiver condition
A requirement that, if met under the terms, removes or reduces a charge.assess a waiver condition
minimum balance
A required balance threshold under a specified measure and period.check the minimum-balance condition
average daily balance
A balance measure averaging daily balances over a defined period.calculate average daily balance
qualifying deposit
A deposit meeting the specified conditions for a benefit or waiver.identify a qualifying deposit
billing period
The interval to which charges are attributed.confirm the billing period
posting entry
A recorded account transaction or charge.trace the posting entry
account conversion
A change from one account product or arrangement to another.review an account conversion
change notice
Communication of altered terms under the applicable process.retrieve the change notice
fee assessment
The application of a charge under specified rules and facts.examine the fee assessment
eligibility
Whether conditions for a particular term or benefit are met.verify waiver eligibility
retroactive application
Applying terms to a period before their stated applicability.question retroactive application
historical schedule
The charge schedule applicable to a past period.obtain the historical schedule
service record
Documentation of customer contacts and actions.update the service record
complaint reference
An identifier linking a complaint to its handling record.provide a complaint reference
refund decision
A determination about returning a charged amount.explain the refund decision
account credit
An entry increasing or offsetting the customer's account balance as applicable.verify an account credit
case resolution
The supported outcome and completion status of a customer case.document case resolution
further review
Additional examination through the applicable process.explain further-review options

A waiver may depend on a specific balance measure, deposit type, or other condition. Do not substitute an end-of-month balance for an average-daily-balance requirement or assume every deposit qualifies. Use the actual applicable terms.

C Notice the language

Take the question seriously before defending the charge

A representative may reach for the current schedule because it is convenient. Restate the actual charge and period, then check the terms that applied. Acknowledge uncertainty without dismissing the complaint or promising a refund before the relevant records are examined.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Restate the charge
You are asking about the $15 maintenance charge for June.
Name the available document
I have a schedule effective from 1 August.
Limit its use
That schedule does not automatically explain the June charge.
Check the product
I need to confirm the account product held during that cycle.
Find the applicable terms
We will retrieve the fee schedule effective for June.
Review the waiver
We also need to check whether a waiver condition applied.
Avoid a premature defense
I have not established that the charge was correct.
Avoid a premature refund
The $5 difference does not by itself establish a refund.
Separate balance measures
The relevant balance measure depends on the actual terms.
Preserve the history
An account conversion may affect which product terms apply.
Check disclosure
Retrieve the relevant change notice if the terms changed.
Make the next step concrete
I will obtain the historical account and fee records.
Give the next contact
I will update you Tuesday at 15:00.
Limit the appointment
That is a review update, not a guaranteed credit date.
Keep the complaint open
The fee question remains unresolved.
Close with accountability
The response will explain the applicable period, product, and basis for the outcome.
6 language and precision notes
Effective from
Establishes when terms apply, not every earlier period.
For June
Connects the complaint to its actual statement cycle.
Held during
Recognizes that the product may have changed over time.
Whether a waiver applied
Requires evidence of eligibility, not an assumed benefit.
Difference versus error
Two different amounts do not automatically prove a billing mistake.
Update versus credit
A communication appointment does not establish an account adjustment.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which first reply is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. I will check the June product, historical terms, and waiver conditions before deciding the charge.

The accurate reply identifies the relevant period and facts without using current terms to invent a historical conclusion.

2. Why does the 1 August date matter?

Answer and explanation

D. It limits the current schedule's applicability and does not automatically establish June terms.

Effective dates help identify which terms apply; the current document alone does not establish the earlier cycle's rules.

3. Which waiver assumption is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

C. Any deposit automatically satisfies every qualifying-deposit condition.

Qualifying deposits are defined by the actual terms, so not every deposit necessarily satisfies the condition.

4. What should Tuesday's update do if the review remains incomplete?

Answer and explanation

A. Identify the actual outstanding records and review status.

The commitment is to communicate the real status, not manufacture an outcome before the evidence is complete.

E Conversations

The current fee schedule may not explain a past charge

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Priya: Customer / Daniel: Customer-service representative

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • service record
  • statement cycle
  • historical terms
  • refund decision
  • account conversion
  • Average daily balance
  • qualifying deposit
  • account product
